\bigskip
\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Source 16: Three mass units with a single unit symbol}
\noindent{\large\bfseries Source 16 (batch-80 manuscript 05): Three mass units with a single unit symbol}\par
\noindent\emph{Decidability theorem and executable checks}, 3 October 2026. Its abstract follows; the two bracketed notes in it are \wtag.
\begin{quote}\small
For every fixed one-dimensional deterministic conservative cellular automaton
with a finite alphabet, positive integer non-vacuum weights, and at most one
weight-one symbol, the complete timed evolution relation at total mass at most
three is effectively Presburger-definable in all input and output positions.
Exact reachability and finite-pattern occurrence, at a fixed position or
anywhere, are decidable. In the stationary-unit frame, every bounded two-unit
cluster remains bounded and has finite-phase motion; three-unit encounters
then reduce to a finite core. Composition with a canonical arithmetic compiler
gives horizon-free quartic certificates with zero or one natural witness tuple.
Consequently, standard nonnegative integer-state number-conserving CA cannot
realize undecidable finite halt-pattern occurrence with three numerical
particles. This separates them from typed weighted three-mass constructions [such as that of Part~\ref{smc:tm:part} (source~14), which uses many weight-one types].
Kong's June 2021 thesis already proves binary three-particle nonuniversality
using a periodic-orbit decomposition. The present weighted, uniform arithmetic
strengthening is stated explicitly; its novelty and the current mass-four
boundary remain unresolved here. [Added 2 October 2026: the mass-four boundary is settled within the same class by source~17 (Theorem~\ref{smc:fm:thm:main}).]
\end{quote}

\section{Model and main result}\label{smc:su:sec:model}

Let $A$ be a finite alphabet with a distinguished vacuum $0$ and a weight
$w:A\to\NN$, where $w(0)=0$ and $w(a)>0$ for every $a\ne0$.
Let $F:A^{\Z}\to A^{\Z}$ be a deterministic, time-independent,
translation-equivariant CA of finite radius $R$. The vacuum is quiescent.
For every finite-support configuration $c$, assume
\[
 M(F(c))=M(c),\qquad M(c)=\sum_{i\in\Z}w(c_i).
\]
There is at most one symbol $u$ with $w(u)=1$. Other symbols may share
weights, including weights two and three. No reversibility assumption is made.
The vacuum is the only \emph{zero-weight} symbol; other constant configurations
are allowed to be fixed.

Write $\tau_a c$ for translation of a configuration by $a$ sites to the right.
A sparse configuration is specified by a strictly increasing position tuple
$x_1<\cdots<x_n$ and its nonzero label tuple $\alpha=(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_n)$.
At mass at most three, $n\le3$, and only finitely many label tuples are possible.

\begin{theorem}[Uniform timed evolution]\label{smc:su:thm:main}
For each fixed $F$ satisfying these assumptions and each pair of nonzero label
tuples $\alpha,\beta$ of total weight at most three, an algorithm constructs a
Presburger formula
\[
 \Rel^F_{\alpha,\beta}(x_1,\ldots,x_n,t,y_1,\ldots,y_m)
\]
which holds exactly when $t\in\NN$, both position tuples are strictly increasing,
and $F^t(c_{\alpha,x})=c_{\beta,y}$. The vacuum is handled as a separate case.
The construction is effective from the finite local rule, radius, and weights,
under the stated hypotheses.
\end{theorem}

``Uniform'' means that the formula includes all sparse input positions,
output positions, and times for one fixed rule. It does not assert a single
Presburger formula in which arbitrary CA rule tables are themselves encoded
as an additional numerical parameter. The effective equivalence with semilinear sets is classical~\cite{ginsburgspanier}.
Presburger arithmetic here is first-order
arithmetic over $\Z$ with addition, order, and integer constants. Quantification
over $\NN$ is expressed by nonnegativity constraints.

\begin{corollary}\label{smc:su:cor:decision}
For any such fixed $F$, the following problems are decidable on finite inputs
of mass at most three:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Exact finite-configuration reachability, including specified-time
reachability and reachability up to translation.
\item Occurrence of a given finite pattern at a specified position, or at some
position, at some nonnegative time.
\item Any fixed finite collection of such observations at specified relative
time offsets.
\end{enumerate}
For fixed input and fixed observation, its set of occurrence times is effectively
semilinear and hence eventually periodic.
\end{corollary}

\wtag\ [The mass-two case is Theorems~\ref{smc:sl:thm:two} and~\ref{smc:sl:thm:semilinear} of Part~IV, proved there for any number of weight-one symbols. Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:walker} and Sections~\ref{smc:su:sec:core}--\ref{smc:su:sec:observation} follow the architecture of Part~IV's Sections~\ref{smc:sl:sec:two}--\ref{smc:sl:sec:semilinearity} (first entry with the absolute position preserved, finite templates after entry, no product $k_0n$), one mass higher. Source~16 cites Part~IV only for its compiler (Section~\ref{smc:su:sec:quartic}).]

\section{The stationary frame and two-unit clusters}\label{smc:su:sec:frame}

If no unit symbol exists, every nonempty configuration of mass at most three
has exactly one occupied site. Conservation forces its successor to have exactly
one site of the same weight. There are finitely many labels and bounded
one-step displacements, so this is a finite-state walker. Its motion is
effectively eventually periodic up to translation, and Theorem~\ref{smc:su:thm:main}
follows immediately. Henceforth assume $u$ exists.

Conservation forces $F(u\text{ at }0)$ to be exactly one copy of $u$, at some
site $\delta\in[-R,R]$. Define
\[
 G=\tau_{-\delta}\circ F,\qquad S=\max\{1,R+|\delta|\}.
\]
Then $G$ has radius at most $S$, is conservative, and fixes each isolated copy
of $u$. Moreover $F^t=\tau_{\delta t}\circ G^t$. It suffices to prove the theorem
for $G$; replacing each output position $y_j$ by $y_j-\delta t$ gives the formula
for $F$.
If the comoving rule has radius zero, each occupied coordinate stays fixed and
its label follows a finite weight-preserving map; finite transient and periodic
label conditions prove the result directly. The padding $S\ge1$ also includes
this case in the argument below without dividing by a zero radius.

Connect two occupied sites if their distance is at most $2S$. Components always
refer to this graph. If distinct components are separated by more than $2S$,
no radius-$S$ input neighborhood sees both. Quiescence therefore gives exact
independence for one step: the global successor is the disjoint union of the
successors of those components evolved alone. Their radius-$S$ output
neighborhoods are disjoint as well.

\begin{lemma}[Two-unit diameter invariance]\label{smc:su:lem:invariant}
If a mass-two configuration has diameter at most $2S$, then every iterate under
$G$ has diameter at most $2S$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
There are only two possibilities. A single weight-two symbol has its successor
inside an interval of width $2S$ by finite propagation.

Otherwise the configuration is $u$ at sites $0$ and $d$, where
$1\le d\le2S$. If $d>S$, each original site has exactly its isolated-unit
radius-$S$ neighborhood. Both original sites therefore output $u$.
These two sites already exhaust the conserved mass, so the entire
configuration is fixed.

If $d\le S$, consider an output site other than $0$ and $d$. If its neighborhood
sees no unit, it outputs zero by quiescence. If it sees just one unit, its local
input agrees with an isolated-unit configuration at that output site; since
isolated units are fixed, it again outputs zero. A new nonzero site must see
both units, and thus lies in
\[
 [d-S,S].
\]
This interval also contains $0$ and $d$, and has width $2S-d\le2S$.
All output support is therefore contained in it. Induction proves the claim.
\end{proof}

This lemma excludes a potentially misleading geometric scenario. Under a
coarse propagation bound alone, a pair could appear to split, let one fragment
join a third unit, and leave the other behind without a full encounter.
Here that cannot happen across distinct $2S$-components: a mass-two component
never splits into two such components. The unique stationary unit label and
mass conservation are essential to this conclusion.
\wtag\ [This no-split lemma is the decisive new ingredient of source~16. It has no counterpart in Part~IV, where several weight-one types let a near pair separate into two walkers (Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:two}).]

\begin{lemma}[Finite phase motion]\label{smc:su:lem:walker}
For each normalized mass-two configuration $q$ of diameter at most $2S$, its
isolated orbit has a computable finite transient and a translated periodic tail.
There exist $\mu\ge0$, $p\ge1$, $D\in\Z$, and explicitly computed finite
configurations $q_0,\ldots,q_{\mu+p-1}$ such that
\[
 G^{\mu+r+np}(q)=\tau_{nD}q_{\mu+r}
 \quad(0\le r<p,\ n\ge0).
\]
Each configuration on this orbit has diameter at most $2S$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Normalize every nonempty configuration by moving its leftmost occupied site to
zero. Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:invariant} leaves finitely many normalized mass-two shapes:
one for each weight-two symbol, and one pair $u,u$ at each distance
$1,\ldots,2S$. The successor shape and change in the leftmost position depend
only on the current shape. Iterating this finite deterministic transition system
reaches a repeated shape. The repeat supplies $\mu,p,D$ and the stated identity
by translation equivariance.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ [Same argument as Proposition~\ref{smc:sl:prop:bounded} and~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:translate-tail} of Part~IV, applied to the finitely many shapes left by Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:invariant}.]

If $N_2$ denotes the number of weight-two labels, there are exactly $N_2+2S$
possible normalized shapes. No speed bound on the movement of the leftmost
occupied site is needed: its displacement on a shape transition is simply
computed from the rule. A mass-two input of diameter greater than $2S$ is two
independent unit symbols and is fixed by $G$.

\section{The bounded encounter core}\label{smc:su:sec:core}

For mass exactly three, define the encounter core by the diameter condition
\[
 \Core=\{c:M(c)=3,\ \diam(\supp c)\le4S\}.
\]
The core includes every connected mass-three configuration, and may include
some disconnected ones. Its use requires no claim that a configuration entering
it has already undergone a local interaction. Modulo translation, the core is
finite. If $N_3$ is the number of weight-three labels, its number of normalized
shapes is
\[
 N_3+8S N_2+\binom{4S}{2}.
\]
These are respectively one weight-three site, a weight-two site together with
a unit site, and three unit sites. One step from the core has diameter at most
$6S$, by finite propagation.

Every mass-three configuration outside the core has one of two forms:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Three mass-one components, each an isolated $u$. They are mutually
separated by more than $2S$ and the entire configuration is fixed by $G$.
\item One mass-two component of diameter at most $2S$, and one isolated $u$.
\end{enumerate}
A weight-three singleton is always in the core. Thus these cases exhaust all
possibilities, including an initial weight-two singleton far from a unit.

\begin{lemma}[Exact evolution before the core]\label{smc:su:lem:outside}
Suppose an outside-core configuration consists of a mass-two component
$\tau_a q$ and a unit at $b$. Let
\[
 z_k=\tau_a G^k(q)
\]
be the isolated mass-two evolution. Consider the counterfactual union of its
support with the fixed site $b$, and let $h$ be the first $k\ge0$ at which the
span of that union is at most $4S$, if there is such a $k$.
Before time $h$, and also at time $h$ when finite, the actual orbit is exactly
$z_k$ together with the unit at $b$. The union at those times has disjoint
occupied sites. If $h$ does not exist, this describes the entire orbit.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:invariant}, $\diam z_k\le2S$. If $b$ were within $2S$ of
any occupied site of $z_k$, the whole union would have span at most $4S$.
Therefore, whenever the counterfactual span is greater than $4S$, the mass-two
component and the unit are separated by more than $2S$. Their next step is
independent and their outputs are disjoint. Induction proves equality up to and
including the first core time. Although a later counterfactual superposition
could have overlapping sites, no such overlap occurs in the part used here.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Effective first entry]\label{smc:su:lem:first}
The first entry in Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:outside}, or the fact that no entry occurs,
is computable without stepping through the initial gap.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The finite transient from Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:walker} is checked directly. On each
periodic phase, write $k=\mu+r+np$ with $n\ge0$; the leftmost site is
$a+A_r+nD$, and the diameter is a constant $d_r\le2S$. Core membership is
exactly the pair of inequalities
\begin{equation}
 b-4S\ \le\ a+A_r+nD\ \le\ b+4S-d_r.                 \label{smc:su:eq:hit}
\end{equation}
For a fixed phase this is an integer interval condition on $n$. If $D=0$ it is
always true or always false. If $D>0$, compute the first nonnegative integer
above the lower bound and check the upper bound; if $D<0$, negate the
inequalities. Take the earliest time among the finitely many valid phase and
transient candidates.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ [Lemmas~\ref{smc:su:lem:outside} and~\ref{smc:su:lem:first} are the exact acceleration to the next encounter of Part~IV (\eqref{smc:sl:eq:encounter} and the paragraph after it), with a bounded mass-two packet in place of a walker. The core and its shape count are new.]

\section{Finite encounter dynamics and complete orbits}\label{smc:su:sec:orbits}

Starting from any normalized core shape, take one exact CA step. If the output
is again in the core, this gives a transition with elapsed time one and a
computed translation. If it is outside, apply the classification above and
Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:first}. Either there is no return, or the first return gives
another core shape with a positive integer elapsed time and an integer
translation. All these data are effective, and depend only on the starting
normalized shape.

This produces a finite deterministic graph of core shapes. Every outgoing
edge either reaches a core shape with a fixed time/translation increment, or
enters a completely described nonreturning orbit. There are two terminal forms:
a fixed configuration, or the independent combination of a stationary unit
and the finite-phase mass-two walker.

\begin{proposition}[Eventual orbit structure]\label{smc:su:prop:orbits}
From each fixed finite configuration of mass at most three, the complete orbit
under $G$ is effectively eventually of one of the following forms:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The entire configuration is periodic up to a single translation:
there are $T\ge0$, $P\ge1$, $d\in\Z$ with
$G^{t+P}(c)=\tau_d G^t(c)$ for all $t\ge T$.
\item A stationary unit and a mass-two component evolve independently,
with the latter periodic up to translation after a finite transient.
\end{enumerate}
The fixed configuration case is included in the first form with $P=1,d=0$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
An initially unbounded separation is resolved by Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:first}, or gives
an immediate nonreturning description. After a core entry, there are finitely
many normalized core shapes. If a nonreturning edge is taken, its description
gives the second form or a fixed configuration. Otherwise a normalized core
shape repeats: at two times $t_0<t_1$ the actual configurations satisfy
$G^{t_1}(c)=\tau_d G^{t_0}(c)$. Determinism and translation equivariance imply
\[
 G^{t_1+n}(c)=\tau_d G^{t_0+n}(c)\qquad(n\ge0).
\]
This identity covers every intermediate configuration, including every part
of every subsequent excursion, not just the core-return times.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ [Same argument as Part~IV's finite near-shape recurrence (\eqref{smc:sl:eq:near} and the paragraphs after it), with the mass-three core in place of the near shapes.]

For a fixed starting configuration, these descriptions immediately yield a
Presburger relation between time and all occupied output positions. A phase
$t=T+r+nP$ gives constant labels and positions of the form $c_j+nd_j$.
The drifts $d_j$ are either all equal, or are the walker drift together with
zero for the stationary unit. Any finite initial segment can be included by a
finite disjunction. Positions, labels, and finite-pattern observations at
intermediate times are retained by this construction.

This proves a per-input semilinearity statement. The next section supplies the
additional step needed for one formula covering arbitrarily large initial gaps.

\section{Uniform Presburger construction}\label{smc:su:sec:uniform}

For every normalized core shape $c$, the preceding finite computation gives
an effective formula $K_c(a,t,y)$ describing the whole orbit from $\tau_a c$.
There are finitely many such formulas. This notation suppresses the finitely
many output-label cases; all displayed formulas may be expanded separately for
each label tuple.

The mass-zero, mass-one, and mass-two cases were already handled by finite phase
motion and separated stationary units. Initial core configurations are covered
by the formulas $K_c$. Initial configurations with three mass-one components
are fixed in the stationary frame.

It remains to treat the outside-core pair-plus-unit case, with bounded
normalized pair shape $q$, pair translation $a$, and unit position $b$.
There are only finitely many choices of $q$. Its isolated profile from
Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:walker} is uniformly Presburger in $(a,k)$: each periodic phase
has time $k=\mu+r+np$ and positions obtained by adding $a+nD$ to fixed constants.
Its finite transient contributes finitely many cases.

Let $H_q(a,b,k)$ express that the span of the counterfactual support together
with $b$ is at most $4S$. Formula~\eqref{smc:su:eq:hit} and its finite transient cases
show that $H_q$ is Presburger. Define its first-hit predicate by
\[
 E_q(a,b,k)\ \equiv\ k\ge0\ \wedge\ H_q(a,b,k)
 \ \wedge\ \neg\exists j\bigl(0\le j<k\ \wedge\ H_q(a,b,j)\bigr).
\]
Presburger arithmetic is closed under these quantifiers and Boolean operations.

For output time $t$, use the union of two cases:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{No entry through time $t$.} Require $t\ge0$ and
$\neg\exists j(0\le j\le t\wedge H_q(a,b,j))$. Describe the output as the
isolated pair profile at time $t$ together with the unit at $b$.
\item \emph{Entry by time $t$.} Existentially choose $0\le k\le t$ satisfying
$E_q(a,b,k)$. For each normalized core shape $c$, express that the entry
configuration is $\tau_e c$, for some integer $e$. Then require
$K_c(e,t-k,y)$.
\end{enumerate}
The entry-shape condition is Presburger: its labels and relative positions
belong to a finite list, while the pair-profile positions, $b$, and the
translation $e$ are related by linear equalities. The minimum-position choice
for $e$, disjoint support, and ordering can all be expressed by finite
conjunctions or disjunctions. The post-entry period and drift depend only on
the finitely chosen core shape, not on the unbounded initial gap or first-entry
time; this introduces no product of two quantified variables. Lemma~\ref{smc:su:lem:outside} guarantees that these are
valid configurations at the first entry.

Every initial label/position case reduces to one of the listed cases through
finitely many comparisons and bounded-offset equalities. The result is a
Presburger formula for $G$. Substituting $y_j-\delta t$ gives the formula for
$F$, completing Theorem~\ref{smc:su:thm:main}.

\wtag\ [Same architecture as Part~IV's linear guard, first entry and templates (\eqref{smc:sl:eq:firstentry} and~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:templates}: ``There is no product $k_0n$''). New here is the composition of a bounded mass-two packet with a distant stationary unit.]

\section{Observation and computational consequences}\label{smc:su:sec:observation}

Exact reachability is obtained by existentially quantifying $t$. Reachability
up to translation also quantifies one spatial shift. To test a finite pattern
$p$ at position $z$, quantify the at-most-three output positions and labels,
and require the specified state at every pattern site. In particular, a zero
in the pattern means that no occupied output position is present at that site;
zeros are not ignored. The finite union over possible output label tuples is
Presburger. Quantify $t$ and, for occurrence anywhere, $z$.
A finite collection of relative-time observations uses a conjunction of the
same timed relation at $t+d_1,\ldots,t+d_k$, with independent output position
tuples. All these constructions are effective.
\wtag\ [Same tests as Part~IV's at every intermediate time (Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:two}), written in Presburger arithmetic.]

\begin{corollary}[Numerical particle lower bound]\label{smc:su:cor:numerical}
Let $F$ be a fixed one-dimensional finite-alphabet number-conserving CA with
nonnegative integer states and weight $w(q)=q$. From configurations whose
numerical state sum is at most three, finite-pattern occurrence and exact
finite-configuration reachability are decidable.
Consequently such a mass-three family cannot encode arbitrary Turing-machine
halting through a computable finite-configuration encoder and a designated
finite halt-pattern occurrence event.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
An integer-state alphabet has at most one state of numerical value one, and
all its nonzero states have positive integer weight. The theorem applies. An
effective reduction of undecidable halting to the stated decidable observation
problem would be a contradiction.
\end{proof}

The conclusion concerns ordinary effective computation with finite input
encodings and finite halt observations. It is not a claim about intrinsically
universal simulation, noncomputable output interpretation, signed conserved
quantities, infinite alphabets, spatially nonuniform rules, time-dependent
rules, or active zero-weight backgrounds. Those are different models.

Multiple distinct weight-one symbols invalidate the stationary-unit argument:
isolated particles can carry finite internal states or have different motions.
A three-mass construction in such a typed weighted alphabet therefore does not
contradict Corollary~\ref{smc:su:cor:numerical}. \wtag\ [Part~\ref{smc:tm:part} (source~14) is such a construction.] Conversely, simply renumbering those
unit symbols as distinct positive integer states cannot preserve their unit
weights. The precise distinction is the number of available labels of mass one,
not the number of geometrically occupied sites.

\section{Horizon free canonical quartic certificates}\label{smc:su:sec:quartic}

The uniform relation has a direct Diophantine consequence. Encode a signed
coordinate $x$ by natural inputs $(x^+,x^-)$ with
\[
 x=x^+-x^-,\qquad x^+=0\ \text{or}\ x^-=0.
\]
This is a unique representation of each integer. Include its domain condition,
the ordering constraints, and $t\ge0$ in the Presburger predicate. Apply effective
Presburger quantifier elimination, for example Cooper's procedure~\cite{cooper},
to obtain a quantifier-free predicate on the chosen natural inputs.
For untimed reachability, eliminate time as well. Leaving a reaching time as an
auxiliary witness would generally destroy uniqueness, because a target may be
visited at several or infinitely many times.

Use the canonical quantifier-free Presburger-to-quartic compiler in
Section 9 of the companion report \textup{[Part~IV of this report (source~13), Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:presburger-quartic}]}. Its precise interface is as
follows. Normalize atoms to affine inequalities $L\ge0$ and fixed-modulus
congruences $L\equiv0\pmod d$, and choose an AND/OR/NOT circuit. Let $I,C,B$
be its actual counts of inequality atoms, congruence atoms, and Boolean gates.
The compiler gives an integer polynomial, a sum of squares of quadratic
residuals, with degree at most four and
\begin{equation}
 K=2I+6C+B\quad\text{natural witnesses},\qquad
 E=2I+5C+B+1\quad\text{residuals}.                 \label{smc:su:eq:counts}
\end{equation}
(The companion report calls the gate count $G$; $B$ avoids confusion with the
stationary-frame CA in this article.) Its natural witness fiber is a singleton
when the predicate is true and empty when false. The atom encodings assign
canonical truth bits and slack or Euclidean-division data, and the acyclic
Boolean circuit has uniquely determined outputs; the final residual requires
the output bit to equal one. The established compiler is reused here rather
than replaced by a new implementation.
\wtag\ [The signed encoding and the elimination of time are those of Part~IV's Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:semilinearity}, last subsection, and~\eqref{smc:su:eq:counts} is~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:presburger-counts}.]

\begin{corollary}[Canonical quartics at mass three]\label{smc:su:cor:quartic}
For every fixed CA and fixed input/output label case in
Theorem~\ref{smc:su:thm:main}, timed and untimed exact reachability admit effectively
constructible integer polynomials of degree at most four with zero or one
natural auxiliary witness tuple. Their witness arity is the formula-dependent
constant $K$ in~\eqref{smc:su:eq:counts}, after quantifier elimination and a chosen Boolean circuit.
No supplied time horizon is required. The same conclusion applies to each
fixed finite-pattern observation predicate from Corollary~\ref{smc:su:cor:decision}.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{smc:su:thm:main}, the stated natural input encoding, quantifier
elimination, and the canonical compiler in that order. The first three steps
preserve the predicate; the last step has the asserted degree, counts, and
natural-fiber property.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ [Corollary~\ref{smc:su:cor:quartic} is Part~IV's Corollary~\ref{smc:sl:cor:fixedarity} one mass higher, with up to twelve rather than eight external natural inputs.]

For exact reachability there are at most twelve external natural coordinate
inputs, and thirteen when time is retained. Thus total arity is at most
$12+K$ untimed or $13+K$ timed for fixed labels. The formula-dependent quantity
$K$ is not a universal numerical arity bound. Quantifier-elimination cost,
formula size, circuit size, integer coefficients and their bit lengths remain
paid parts of any concrete instantiation; no efficient preprocessing or optimal
arity is claimed. The executable package validates the orbit accelerator, not
a general-purpose rule-to-formula or quantifier-elimination implementation.

Uniqueness comes from post-elimination arithmetic, not merely from deterministic
CA evolution. No corresponding exactness of the nonnegative-real or rational
witness fiber is claimed. This corollary concerns the decidable single-unit-label
class and gives no finite-fold conclusion for arbitrary recursively enumerable
sets or for a universal typed three-mass construction.

\wtag\ \textbf{Added 2 October 2026 (batch 80).} Two sharper compilers apply. The programme's five-witness congruence atom~\cite{reviewcong}, already recorded for Part~IV (Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:reproduction}), applies verbatim after elimination and gives $K=2I+5C+B$ natural witnesses at the same $E$, keeping the single-fold property and degree four; source~16's provenance record says that it retains the verified six-witness bound, and the programme's review of this source~\cite{reviewlowmass} identifies the substitution as a formula-dependent saving, with no total-gate claim. Theorem \texttt{cdc:of:thm:singlefoldsl} of Part~XI of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates} gives the same semilinear relation an orthant-nonnegative single-fold representation of degree two, as the note after Part~IV's Corollary~\ref{smc:sl:cor:fixedarity} says for mass two. No novelty is claimed for Corollary~\ref{smc:su:cor:quartic}.

\section{Primary sources and what remains unresolved}\label{smc:su:sec:sources}

There is a direct primary-source precursor. Gil-Tak Kong's June 2021
thesis~\cite{kongthesis}, Section 2.4.1, Theorem 2, printed page 14 (PDF page 15),
proves non-strong-Turing-universality with three particles. Its definitions are
explicitly one-dimensional and binary: Definition 1, printed page 8 (PDF page 9),
uses alphabet $\{0,1\}$. The proof distinguishes eventual periodicity up to
translation of the whole configuration from eventual decomposition into
independent periodic components. Thus binary three-particle nonuniversality
and this orbit-decomposition approach have an explicit 2021 precursor.

The present article states and proves a weighted extension with at most one
unit label, effective uniform timed Presburger formulas in all sparse positions,
exact and finite-pattern observation consequences, and the canonical quartic
composition. These are the precise claims to compare with earlier work;
we do not claim that the brief sources establish or exclude these stronger
statements, or that their absence from a short source would establish novelty.

Imai's KAKEN final report dated 25 June 2021~\cite{kakenfinal}, PDF page 4,
Section 4, item (2), also reports that motion representation was used to establish
decidability when the particle number is at most three. The Japanese sentence
leaves the decision problem implicit; it does not identify exact reachability,
pattern occurrence, an input encoding, or a complexity bound. This establishes a
numerical-NCCA decidability precursor, while leaving its relation to the fully
specified weighted timed claim here undetermined. PDF page 3 distinguishes the need to
encode NCCA internal state geometrically from the capabilities of pebble
automata and discusses the earlier five-particle construction.

The publisher abstract of Alhazov and Imai~\cite{alhazovimai} describes
finite-support, nonnegative-integer-state NCCAs. It reports a radius-one
universal construction via two-register machines and a universal construction
with five particles when a sufficiently large finite radius is allowed.
The accessible abstract does not claim minimality. The full paper was not
accessible in this check, so its detailed discussion of smaller particle counts
could not be verified.

A Kyoto University RIMS report~\cite{kongprogram}, printed page 93, records
Kong and Imai's presentation on particle complexity on 2 February 2021;
the KAKEN project record~\cite{kaken} places that title under academic year 2020.
The thesis above supplies a directly inspectable mathematical source.

Kong's thesis conclusion, printed page 15 (PDF page 16), describes the
four-particle case as open in 2021. That is a historical statement, not evidence
that the question remains open today. The current article proves insufficiency
of three for the stated observation task in its weighted single-unit-label
class, while the 2016 abstract supplies a five-particle numerical upper bound.
It does not settle the current four-versus-five boundary or the novelty of its
explicit weighted, uniform Presburger, or Diophantine strengthenings.

\wtag\ \textbf{Added 2 October 2026 (batch 80).} The four-versus-five boundary is settled within this weighted single-unit class by source~17 (Part~\ref{smc:su:part}): exact reachability and pattern occurrence are decidable at mass four (Theorem~\ref{smc:fm:thm:main}), so the numerical lower bound is five (Corollary~\ref{smc:fm:cor:numerical}); a five-particle upper bound rests on the Alhazov--Imai abstract only. The programme's review~\cite{reviewlowmass} read the cited pages of Kong's thesis (the binary Definition~1, Theorem~2, and the four-particle case open in 2021) and retrieved Imai's KAKEN report, whose Japanese text it could not extract and does not certify; it obtained no full text of the Alhazov--Imai paper.

Two further directions remain outside this proof: classifying the effect of two
or more unit labels, including the least unit-label count permitting a universal
three-mass construction; and deriving quantitative bounds for encounter
acceleration, Presburger elimination, and the resulting quartic ledger. \wtag\ [Both are open; they are also source~17's Questions~\ref{smc:fm:q:severalunits} and~\ref{smc:fm:q:complexity}.]

\section{Executable checks and limits of validation}\label{smc:su:sec:checks}

The accompanying Python script implements the finite mass-two phase profile,
exact first-core acceleration for positive, zero, and negative drift, and
whole-orbit acceleration using the $4S$ core. It compares accelerated
configurations against direct simulation, including all intermediate sampled
times. The tests use:
\begin{itemize}
\item Sixteen binary conservative radius-two rules obtained from disjoint
parallel swaps of selected $10$ pairs, in both directions. The selection is
false for an isolated unit, so its drift is zero.
\item All five conservative elementary binary rules, 170, 184, 204, 226,
and 240, each shifted into its unique-unit stationary frame. Their conservation
is certified exactly by a potential on the four-vertex de Bruijn graph; all 256
elementary rules are tested against that finite identity.
\item An ordinary four-state NCCA with numerical states $0,1,2,3$, using
locally guarded translation, a $2+1$ merge into $3$, and a reverse split.
Its label values equal its weights.
\item A locally guarded weighted rewrite CA with a unique unit label,
several weight-two labels, and two weight-three labels. Its cases include
positive and negative walkers, zero-drift phase motion, merging, splitting
inside a bounded cluster, recurrent encounters, and terminal separation.
\end{itemize}
Each sample rule has a direct conservation argument: binary moves swap
disjoint occupied/empty pairs; weighted rewrites have disjoint guarded supports
and preserve the mass on each rewritten support. The code also tests dense,
multi-center configurations as a consistency check.

The recorded run passed 7,127 orbit cases and 1,140,320 direct-versus-accelerated
time comparisons across 23 rules. It checked 102 mass-two profiles, 5,103 signed
linear interval cases, and 2,760 dense conservation cases. A binary pair with
period two and drift one was tested against an initial unit at
$10^{30}+17$; the accelerator handled the enormous separation using 13 core
visits, without iterating through the gap.

These counts describe actual CA orbit cases and direct-versus-accelerated
configuration comparisons, not counts of symbolic proof templates.
These finite tests exercise the implementation and the proposed normal forms.
They are neither an exhaustive enumeration of all conservative CA rules nor a
machine-checked proof of the theorem. A separate executable audit
passed 1,186 boundary orbits with 118,600 direct time comparisons, 4,109
independent first-core checks, and 216,160 finite-window conservation cases.
It also tested reflected negative-drift gaps, zero drift, sorting and
translations, with closed-form checks through time $10^{80}+123$; its full
ledger is included in the package. The mathematical argument, including the
no-split lemma, uniform first-entry composition, and all-intermediate-time
cycle identity, was reviewed independently as well.

\wtag\ \textbf{The release in this report.} These are the delivered names. The script and the independent audit are shipped as \path{code/16-single-unit-test_single_unit_acceleration.py} and \path{code/16-single-unit-audit_accelerator.py}, with \path{code/16-single-unit-run-tests.sh} and \path{code/16-single-unit-build.sh}; their receipts as \path{data/16-single-unit-test-results.json} and \path{data/16-single-unit-audit-results.json}; and \path{REVISION.md}, \path{SOURCE-PROVENANCE.md} and \path{independent/AUDIT.md} as \path{16-single-unit-*} at the report root. The manuscript, PDF, README and checksum ledger are not shipped, and nothing of manuscript~06 is (its programs, receipts and audit are the same files). \path{run-tests.sh} copies the two programs to a temporary directory and compares the receipts; it needs the delivered layout (README). The programme's review reran both programs and reproduced both receipts~\cite{reviewlowmass}.

\bigskip
\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Source 17: Four mass units and exact reachability}
\noindent{\large\bfseries Source 17 (batch-80 manuscript 03): Four mass units and exact reachability}\par
\noindent\emph{Decidability proof and a binary timing certificate}, 3 October 2026 (its PDF metadata name the author as ``Mathematical research report''). \wtag\ Its ``companion'' report is source~16, printed above. Its abstract follows.
\begin{quote}\small
For every fixed one-dimensional finite-alphabet conservative cellular automaton
with a unique zero-weight vacuum, positive integer non-vacuum weights, and at
most one weight-one symbol, exact finite-configuration reachability and
finite-pattern occurrence are decidable at total mass at most four.
In the stationary-unit frame, recurrent separated interactions reduce to a
finite-control additive gap counter. A translating three-mass gadget either
escapes or brings all four masses into a bounded encounter; it cannot retain a
second independent gap. The remaining expanding shuttles have spatial phases
affine in their excursion number and quadratic accumulated time. This also
handles anchored observations in the original moving frame.

Timed semilinearity does fail at mass four: an explicit binary radius-six
NCCA has anchored return times $t_k=k^2+(2d-11)k$, $d\ge7$. Its full local
table has an exact de Bruijn conservation certificate. A single squared
quadratic gives a horizon-free quartic timing certificate with exactly one
natural witness at a hit. Primary 2021 lower-particle precursors are credited;
no priority claim or present-day literature-status conclusion is inferred
from an unsuccessful search.
\end{quote}

\section{Model and results}\label{smc:fm:sec:model}
Let $A$ be finite, with a distinguished vacuum $0$, and let
$w:A\to\NN$ satisfy $w(0)=0$ and $w(a)>0$ for $a\ne0$.
Let $F:A^{\Z}\to A^{\Z}$ be a deterministic, time-independent,
translation-equivariant cellular automaton of finite radius $R$. Assume that
$0^{\Z}$ is fixed and that, for every finite-support configuration $c$,
\[
 M(F(c))=M(c),\qquad M(c)=\sum_{i\in\Z}w(c_i).
\]
There is at most one symbol of weight one. Labels of higher weights may be
repeated. Write $\tau_a c$ for translation to the right by $a$ sites.

\begin{theorem}[Four-mass decision theorem]\label{smc:fm:thm:main}
For every fixed $F$ satisfying these assumptions, the following problems are
decidable for finite initial configurations of mass at most four:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Exact reachability of a specified finite configuration, including
reachability up to translation.
\item Occurrence of a specified finite pattern at a specified position, or
anywhere, at some nonnegative time.
\end{enumerate}
The procedures are effective from the finite rule, radius and weights, under
the stated hypotheses. No bound on their computational efficiency is asserted.
\end{theorem}

A pattern may contain zeros, and these are enforced. The theorem concerns
finite inputs and finite observations. It does not cover infinite initial
backgrounds, signed weights, an infinite alphabet, an active zero-weight
background, multiple unit labels, or time-dependent or spatially nonuniform
rules.

\begin{corollary}[Ordinary numerical particles]\label{smc:fm:cor:numerical}
A fixed one-dimensional finite-alphabet NCCA whose states are nonnegative
integers and whose weights equal those integers cannot encode arbitrary
Turing-machine halting by a computable finite-configuration encoder of total
numerical mass at most four and a designated finite halt-pattern observation.
The same conclusion holds for exact-target halting observations.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
There is at most one numerical state equal to one. Such an encoding would reduce
an undecidable problem to a decision problem in Theorem~\ref{smc:fm:thm:main}.
\end{proof}

The 2016 publisher abstract of Alhazov and Imai reports a universal numerical
NCCA with five particles~\cite{alhazovimai}; the full paper was not available
for this check. The present lower bound matches that reported numerical upper
bound for the usual effective finite-input halting-observation interpretation.
\wtag\ [It would match, if the 2016 construction uses an effective finite input encoding and a finite halt-pattern observation; only its abstract was checked, and source~17's delivery README says ``compatible with''.]
It does not establish a five-particle upper bound for the \emph{binary} subclass.

\section{Stationary units and small packets}\label{smc:fm:sec:packets}
Assume first that the unit symbol $u$ exists. Its isolated successor must be
exactly one $u$, at a fixed displacement $\delta\in[-R,R]$. Put
\[
 G=\tau_{-\delta}F,\qquad S=\max(1,R+|\delta|).
\]
Then $G$ has radius at most $S$, fixes each isolated $u$, and
$F^t=\tau_{\delta t}G^t$. Components below join occupied sites at distance
at most $2S$. If two components are separated by more than $2S$, their next
steps evolve independently and have disjoint output support.

\wtag\ [This section restates the ingredients of source~16 that source~17 needs (Section~\ref{smc:su:sec:frame} and the next two); the mass-two case for any number of weight-one symbols is Part~IV's Theorem~\ref{smc:sl:thm:two}. The proofs of the two lemmas are pointers.]

\begin{lemma}[Bounded mass-two packets]\label{smc:fm:lem:two}
A mass-two configuration of diameter at most $2S$ stays within that diameter
under $G$. Its orbit is effectively eventually periodic up to translation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[\wtag\ Pointer]
This is source~16's Lemmas~\ref{smc:su:lem:invariant} and~\ref{smc:su:lem:walker}, proved there by the same argument (the cases $d>S$ and $d\le S$, with all new support in $[d-S,S]$, then finitely many normalized shapes); not reprinted.
\end{proof}

An isolated packet profile can therefore be written, after a finite transient,
as
\begin{equation}
 G^{\mu+r+kp}(q)=\tau_{kD}q_r,
 \qquad 0\le r<p,\quad k\ge0.                 \label{smc:fm:eq:profile}
\end{equation}
Its labels, phase shapes, period, drift and transient are explicit finite data.
Two units farther than $2S$ apart are fixed.

\begin{lemma}[Effective three-mass profiles]\label{smc:fm:lem:three}
Every fixed mass-three input has an effectively computable finite prefix and
one of these tails:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the whole configuration is periodic up to a single translation;
\item a stationary unit and an independent mass-two packet with finite-phase
motion.
\end{enumerate}
Every intermediate configuration is included in the resulting description.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[\wtag\ Pointer]
This is source~16's Proposition~\ref{smc:su:prop:orbits}, with Lemmas~\ref{smc:su:lem:outside} and~\ref{smc:su:lem:first} for the first entry into the core of diameter $4S$ and the finite graph of core returns; not reprinted.
\end{proof}

For total mass at most three, these profiles already give a fixed-input
timed Presburger description, and the frame translation $\delta t$ preserves
it. Thus all conclusions of Theorem~\ref{smc:fm:thm:main} at mass at most three
are settled here; the remaining component analysis assumes mass exactly four.

This is the small-packet ingredient of the companion mass-three
report \textup{[source~16, Part~\ref{smc:su:part}]}. The present argument does not require its stronger
uniform timed-Presburger conclusion.

\section{A finite library of local encounters}\label{smc:fm:sec:library}
Let $\mathcal E$ be the finite set of normalized connected mass-three
configurations. Every seed has diameter at most $4S$. When a bounded mass-two
packet first comes within $2S$ of a unit, their union is such a seed. At the
first-contact time their supports are still disjoint: they evolved from
components separated by more than $2S$ at the preceding step.

Tag each entry seed by the target unit's coordinate within it. This finite
bookkeeping tag matters when the incoming packet itself consists of two unit
symbols. It adds no physical label; it records which incoming coordinate was
the stationary target.

For each $e\in\mathcal E$, compute Lemma~\ref{smc:fm:lem:three}'s orbit profile and
choose a time $T_e$ giving one of two normalized outcomes:
\begin{description}
\item[Compact outcome.] The complete mass-three configuration has a
translated-periodic tail.
\item[Emission outcome.] A stationary unit at coordinate $a_e$ remains,
and a mass-two packet has nonzero periodic drift and recedes from that unit
forever. Its launch phase and coordinates are fixed relative to the seed.
\end{description}
If an independent packet has zero drift, its support is bounded and periodic,
so reclassify the whole tail as compact. With nonzero drift, prolong the finite
prefix by whole periods until every future packet phase is farther than $2S$
from the stationary unit on the drift side. Earlier detachments and returns are
part of the finite prefix. Infinitely recurrent detachments within a translated
periodic three-mass orbit belong to its compact phase library.

Choose an integer $B\ge4S$ bounding the absolute value of every occupied
coordinate, relative to its normalized seed, in every finite prefix through
$T_e$ and every initial-period phase representative of either outcome.
Include the residual marker coordinate $a_e$. Then compact phase diameters
are at most $2B$, and emitted head-to-marker offsets have magnitude at most
$2B$. Set
\begin{equation}
             N=4B+12S,\qquad W=N+2B.            \label{smc:fm:eq:thresholds}
\end{equation}
These constants are noncircular: $B$ is computed from the original span-$4S$
seed family, not from all mass-three configurations of diameter $W$.

\begin{lemma}[Safe local resolution]\label{smc:fm:lem:safe}
Suppose a configuration consists of $\tau_a e$, $e\in\mathcal E$, and a
fourth unit at $b$, with $|b-a|>B+2S$. The complete isolated seed prefix through
$T_e$ evolves independently of that unit. Subsequently:
\begin{enumerate}
\item a compact outcome either stays independent forever, or first contacts the
fourth unit in a complete configuration of diameter less than $W$;
\item an emission changes the nearby residual marker by a fixed seed-dependent
offset and leaves the fourth unit fixed.
\end{enumerate}
All alternatives and the first-contact times are effectively computable.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
During the prefix the seed support lies in $[a-B,a+B]$, at distance greater
than $2S$ from $b$. Exact independence applies step by step, including its
terminal time. A compact phase has diameter at most $2B$. Its zero drift or
drift away from $b$ gives independence forever. If its drift points toward
$b$, compute first proximity at most $2S$ from its affine phase profiles.
Such a contact exists: finite propagation prevents a packet from crossing the
stationary unit without proximity at most $2S$. At contact the total diameter
is at most $2B+2S<W$. The emission statement is the computed isolated profile.
\end{proof}

An outside-$W$ seed-plus-unit configuration automatically satisfies the safety
condition: the seed diameter is at most $4S$, so
$|b-a|>W-4S>B+2S$. Thus a translating mass-three gadget is not collapsed into
a constant-time local reaction. Its arbitrarily long trip is either terminal
or ends in a bounded full encounter.

\section{The global scattering section}\label{smc:fm:sec:scattering}
Call every complete mass-four configuration of diameter at most $W$ an
\emph{ordinary state}; there are finitely many up to translation.
A \emph{live emission section} consists of two stationary units at $L<R$ and
one bounded mass-two packet at a prescribed launch offset from one endpoint,
with nonzero drift toward the other endpoint. Write $D=R-L>N$. The finite
mode records the source side, emitted packet type and phase, and tagged seed
information. The geometric ordinary and live sets can overlap. We use this
deterministic representation convention: at a designated emission checkpoint,
select the live tag whenever $D>N$, even if the span is at most $W$; at other
dispatch states apply the ordinary span test first. These tags belong to the
analysis and do not add memory to the CA. A live step retains its tag through
the next designated emission or an explicit ordinary reset.

\begin{lemma}[Additive gap transitions]\label{smc:fm:lem:counter}
Every continuing live-to-live transition, for sufficiently large gap as
already ensured by (\ref{smc:fm:eq:thresholds}), has the form
\begin{equation}
 q'=f(q,D\bmod M),\qquad
 D'=D+c,\qquad L'=L+e,\qquad h=\alpha D+\beta,       \label{smc:fm:eq:counter}
\end{equation}
where the constants are selected from a finite effective list,
$c,e\in\Z$, $\alpha>0$ is rational, and $h$ is integer on the permitted
residue class. $M$ is one fixed positive integer. All intermediate
configurations have effective finite-phase descriptions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The emitted packet is permanently independent of the source unit. Follow it
until first proximity at most $2S$ to the target unit at $b$. The resulting
connected mass-three seed has left edge $a'$ with $|a'-b|\le4S$.
The old source marker $p$ therefore satisfies
\[
 |p-a'|\ge D-4S>N-4S>B+2S.
\]
Lemma~\ref{smc:fm:lem:safe} applies to its complete local prefix, however long that
fixed prefix is. For an emission from the new seed, the new stationary marker
is $p'=a'+a_{e'}$, so
\[
 |p'-b|\le4S+B.
\]
Only the contacted marker moves. Since $D>N>B+4S$, marker order cannot
reverse. The new gap and left marker thus change by bounded constants. Unit
identities can switch within the encounter; the new coordinates, not identities,
are what the section records.

If the outgoing packet points outward, the tail is terminal independent
motion. If it points inward with new gap at most $N$, its complete launch
configuration has diameter at most $N+2B=W$ and is ordinary. A compact
outcome is accelerated separately by Lemma~\ref{smc:fm:lem:safe}; it either escapes
or resets to an ordinary state. Carrying a marker inside a translating
three-mass packet cannot preserve an extra unbounded gap through that reset.

For the arithmetic, reflect coordinates if necessary so the packet drift is
$d>0$, with period $p$. In phase $r$, let its left edge be
$p_0+h_r+kd$ and its width be $d_r\le2S$, where $p_0$ is the source marker.
Contact with target $p_0+D$ is exactly
\begin{equation}
 D-2S-d_r\ \le\ h_r+kd\ \le\ D+2S.             \label{smc:fm:eq:contact}
\end{equation}
The hull criterion is exact: because packet width is at most $2S$, the union
of the radius-$2S$ neighborhoods of its occupied sites is the single expanded
hull interval. There is no hole that falsely counts as a contact.

On each residue $\rho\bmod d$, every candidate period count is
$(D-\rho)/d$ plus an integer constant. The choice of earliest valid phase
therefore depends only on $\rho$; all candidate times have leading coefficient
$p/d$. The bound $D>N$ ensures nonnegative candidate counts in the periodic
regime. Finite propagation ensures that one phase is valid. The entry seed and
its tag are fixed relative to the target. Add the fixed local-prefix time to
obtain (\ref{smc:fm:eq:counter}). Take $M$ to be a common multiple of the nonzero
emission drifts. Every precontact phase and every local-prefix configuration
is retained, not merely the transition endpoint.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Complete effective dispatch]\label{smc:fm:lem:dispatch}
From an arbitrary mass-four configuration, one can effectively obtain a
terminal independent profile, an ordinary state, or a live emission section,
with an exact description of the intervening orbit. From an ordinary state,
the next such section can be chosen at positive elapsed time.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The possible $2S$-component mass partitions are
\[
       4,\quad 3+1,\quad 2+2,\quad 2+1+1,\quad 1+1+1+1.
\]
A connected component of mass $m$ has diameter at most $2S(m-1)$.
The first partition is ordinary since $6S<W$; four unit components are fixed.
For $3+1$, an outside-$W$ state has a connected seed and a safely distant
unit, so Lemma~\ref{smc:fm:lem:safe} applies.

For $2+2$, both packets have finite-phase independent motion until first
proximity at most $2S$. Use a common phase period; each signed relative gap
is affine in the period count. Finite transient checks and integer interval
calculations find first contact or prove none occurs. First contact has total
span at most $6S<W$.

For $2+1+1$, compute the packet's first proximity to either stationary unit
by the same finite-phase calculation. If absent, the entire tail is
independent. At contact, a connected three-mass seed is formed. A simultaneous
contact connects all four masses and has span at most $6S$. Otherwise either
the complete configuration is ordinary, or its remaining unit is safely distant
and Lemma~\ref{smc:fm:lem:safe} applies. Before every first contact, independence is
exact; it also holds at the first-contact time itself.

From an ordinary state take one exact step. If still ordinary, retain that
step. Otherwise its span is at most $W+2S$, and the exhaustive dispatch above
applies. Its result is terminal, ordinary, or live. This supplies a positive-time
outgoing section edge for each of the finitely many normalized ordinary states.
Live edges include a nonempty flight. Zero-time section loops do not arise.
\end{proof}

\section{Termination and the expanding shuttle form}\label{smc:fm:sec:termination}
Enlarge the finite live mode by $D\bmod M$. A repeated finite control then has
gap difference divisible by $M$, and consequently the same residue itinerary
on each repeated cycle. Translation $L$ is recorded as output information;
it is not used as another control counter.

\begin{proposition}[Effective orbit alternatives]\label{smc:fm:prop:normal}
For every fixed mass-four input, its $G$-orbit is effectively eventually one of:
\begin{enumerate}
\item independent finite-phase objects;
\item a whole configuration periodic up to translation;
\item an expanding shuttle cycle, whose corresponding-section gap and left
marker are affine in a cycle number $n$, whose complete spatial phases are
Presburger in $n$ and one within-flight period count, and whose accumulated
section time is quadratic in $n$ with positive leading coefficient.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Follow the effective section machine. If an ordinary normalized state repeats,
the complete configurations at those two times differ by a translation.
Determinism gives alternative 2 at every subsequent intermediate time.

During an uninterrupted live excursion, store the finite controls. A control
repeats after finitely many transitions. Let $\Delta$ be the change in gap
between those repeats. If $\Delta=0$, the complete launch configuration repeats
up to translation, again giving alternative 2. If $\Delta>0$, all intermediate
large-gap guards remain valid forever and the same residue itinerary repeats.
This gives alternative 3.

If $\Delta<0$, compute the maximal number of cycle repetitions for which
\emph{every intermediate gap} remains greater than $N$. Fast-forward those
repetitions, summing their constant anchor updates and affine durations, then
execute the finite remaining cycle prefix until the ordinary region is reached.
This is exact finite arithmetic. There are only finitely many ordinary
normalized states, so one cannot visit them indefinitely without repetition.
The section analysis therefore terminates with one of the stated alternatives.

For the expanding case, write the corresponding-section coordinates as
\begin{equation}
 D_n=D_0+n\Delta,\qquad L_n=L_0+nE,\qquad \Delta>0. \label{smc:fm:eq:spatial}
\end{equation}
There are finitely many edges per cycle. A bounded scattering-prefix phase has
positions affine in $n$. A flight phase has marker positions affine in $n$ and
packet positions affine in $(n,j)$, where $j\ge0$ is its packet-period count.
Its allowable range is given by linear inequalities. The finite union of these
phase formulas describes every visited configuration and is Presburger.

Each live edge duration is affine in its incoming gap, with positive leading
coefficient. Consequently the complete cycle duration is affine in $n$ and
strictly increases, and its start time has the form
\begin{equation}
 T_n=T_0+A\frac{n(n-1)}2+C n,\qquad A>0.       \label{smc:fm:eq:time}
\end{equation}
Coefficients are computable rationals yielding integer times. There is a
computable $C_1$ for which every configuration throughout cycle $n$ has support
inside
\begin{equation}
 [L_0+nE-C_1,\quad L_0+nE+D_0+n\Delta+C_1].   \label{smc:fm:eq:bound}
\end{equation}
Local prefixes have uniformly bounded offsets; each free flight stays between
its two markers up to bounded phase deviations. Thus (\ref{smc:fm:eq:bound}) includes
every phase, including all short local transients, rather than just sections.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Untimed spatial description in the stationary frame]\label{smc:fm:cor:spatial}
For each fixed input of mass at most four, the set of configurations visited
by $G$ has an effectively constructible Presburger description in their
occupied coordinates, separately for each possible output-label tuple.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use Proposition~\ref{smc:fm:prop:normal}. Independent finite-phase and translated
periodic tails have affine descriptions. Expanding spatial phases were described
in its proof. All earlier parts are finite, or finitely many explicitly given
finite-phase segments, so can be included effectively. An enormous finite prefix
may be enumerated; no efficiency claim is involved.
\end{proof}

\section{Anchored observations in the original frame}\label{smc:fm:sec:anchored}
Translation-invariant questions, including exact reachability up to translation
and occurrence anywhere, have the same answers for $F$ and $G$. Existentially
quantify the spatial shift in the preceding corollary.

For anchored questions, if the eventual orbit is independent finite-phase or
translated periodic, its fixed-input timed relation is Presburger. Adding
$\delta t$ to all coordinates preserves that property. If the orbit is an
expanding shuttle and $\delta=0$, its untimed spatial description suffices.

Suppose now $\delta\ne0$. For $\delta>0$, throughout cycle $n$ every occupied
coordinate in the $F$-orbit is at least
\[
     \delta T_n+L_0+nE-C_1,
\]
which tends to $+\infty$ by (\ref{smc:fm:eq:time}). For $\delta<0$, every occupied
coordinate is at most
\[
     \delta T_n+L_0+nE+D_0+n\Delta+C_1,
\]
which tends to $-\infty$. These estimates are uniform over the whole cycle
because $t\ge T_n$ and (\ref{smc:fm:eq:bound}) holds throughout.

Given a finite target window, compute an integer cycle index $n_*$ beyond which the
appropriate quadratic bound lies permanently outside it. Simulate or accelerate
the finite prefix up to section $T_{n_*}$. A pattern with a nonzero symbol can occur
only in this prefix; an all-zero pattern occurs afterward. A nonempty exact
target has the same cutoff. The vacuum case is immediate from conservation.
This proves anchored decidability without a general quadratic Diophantine solver
and without claiming timed Presburger definability.

For a pattern, every zero site is enforced by absence of an occupied coordinate
there. Since mass is at most four, only finitely many nonzero label tuples are
possible. Exact configuration equality and these finite pattern tests are thus
effective in all the described cases.

If there is no unit symbol, mass at most four occupies at most two sites.
An isolated site of weight two or three is a finite-state walker; a weight-four
local configuration may split into two weight-two walkers. A bounded encounter
core has finitely many normalized states. Outside it there are two independent
finite-phase walkers, whose first encounter is computed by common-period linear
inequalities. The same finite-core return argument gives an eventually
translated-periodic or independent-two-walker orbit. This handles the missing
case and completes Theorem~\ref{smc:fm:thm:main}.

\section{An explicit binary expanding shuttle}\label{smc:fm:sec:binary}
The loss of timed semilinearity already occurs for an ordinary binary NCCA.
Join consecutive occupied sites whose distance is at most two. On every such
component, apply these replacements up to translation:
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{rcl@{\qquad}l}
\{0,1\}&\longmapsto&\{1,2\}&\text{right-moving pair},\\
\{0,2\}&\longmapsto&\{-1,1\}&\text{left-moving pair},\\
\{0,1,3\}&\longmapsto&\{-1,1,4\}&\text{right bounce},\\
\{0,2,4\}&\longmapsto&\{0,3,4\}&\text{left bounce}.
\end{array}                                                   \label{smc:fm:eq:binary}
\end{equation}
Leave every other component unchanged.

\begin{proposition}[A genuine conservative local rule]\label{smc:fm:prop:rule}
Rule (\ref{smc:fm:eq:binary}) defines a binary conservative CA of radius at most six.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Each replacement preserves the number of occupied sites, and its output hull
extends the input hull by at most one site. Distinct input components have
separation at least three, so these expanded output hulls still have disjoint
lattice sites. This proves global conservation for every finite configuration.

A recognized component is an exact pattern of span at most four, with no
occupied site within two beyond either endpoint. For any output site, every
recognized pattern that can change it, together with both endpoint guards,
is contained in its radius-six neighborhood. All other sites retain their old
value. Thus the component definition is a local rule, including on arbitrarily
large unrecognized components. The vacuum and isolated units are fixed.
\end{proof}

The rule is not injective: the distinct configurations $\{0,1,4,6\}$ and
$\{1,2,3,5\}$ both map to $\{1,2,3,5\}$. No reversibility is asserted.

The package exports all $2^{13}=8192$ local outputs. Independently, a potential
$P$ on the $2^{12}=4096$ de Bruijn vertices satisfies every identity
\begin{equation}
 f(w_0,\ldots,w_{12})-w_6
 =P(w_1,\ldots,w_{12})-P(w_0,\ldots,w_{11}).       \label{smc:fm:eq:potential}
\end{equation}
The complete table and potential are included as a compact JSON certificate.
Summation over the lattice telescopes, yielding another exact conservation
proof, not merely a dense-window test.

\begin{proposition}[Exact quadratic hit times]\label{smc:fm:prop:hits}
From occupied sites $\{0,3,4,d\}$, $d\ge7$, the anchored pattern $10011$
at sites $0,\ldots,4$ occurs exactly at
\begin{equation}
                 t_k=k^2+(2d-11)k,\qquad k\in\NN. \label{smc:fm:eq:hits}
\end{equation}
Its occurrence-time set is not eventually periodic and is not Presburger.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The adjacent pair travels from $\{3,4\}$ to $\{d-3,d-2\}$ in $d-6$ steps.
The component $\{d-3,d-2,d\}$ then becomes $\{d-4,d-2,d+1\}$ in one step.
The left-moving pair returns to $\{2,4\}$ in $d-6$ steps, after which the
component $\{0,2,4\}$ becomes $\{0,3,4\}$. One cycle takes $2d-10$ steps
and increments $d$ by one. Summing cycle durations proves (\ref{smc:fm:eq:hits}).
Between returns the adjacent right-moving pair is strictly farther right,
or the left-moving pair has distance two and cannot occupy $3,4$ together.
There are no extra pattern hits.

Successive hit gaps are $2k+2d-10$, which are unbounded. Every infinite
ultimately periodic subset of $\NN$ has bounded gaps after a finite prefix.
The claimed failure follows; the equivalence of Presburger and semilinear sets
is classical~\cite{ginsburgspanier}.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ [The hypothesis $d\ge7$ is safe but not sharp: a direct simulation of rule~\eqref{smc:fm:eq:binary} at this write, like the placement check, gives exactly the times~\eqref{smc:fm:eq:hits} also for $d=6$, through $t\le2500$, while the formula fails for $d=5$. Section~\ref{smc:fm:sec:quartic} writes $d=7+x$ with natural $x$.]

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=.94\linewidth]{figures/17-four-mass-binary-four-particle-shuttle.pdf}
\caption{An actual trace of the exported binary local rule. The left marker
stays fixed; the right marker moves outward by one after each cycle. The two
remaining units alternate between an adjacent right-moving pair and a
separated left-moving pair. The exact trace data and plotting source accompany
the package.}\label{smc:fm:fig:shuttle}
\end{figure}

\section{A one-witness quartic timing certificate}\label{smc:fm:sec:quartic}
Write $d=7+x$, where $x\in\NN$ is an input. Define
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal P(x,t;k)=\bigl[k^2+(2x+3)k-t\bigr]^2.}          \label{smc:fm:eq:quartic}
\end{equation}
For natural inputs $x,t$, this degree-four polynomial has one natural auxiliary
witness $k$, one squared quadratic residual, and three total variables.
Its natural witness fiber is a singleton exactly when pattern $10011$ occurs
at time $t$, and empty otherwise. Indeed the residual must vanish, and
\[
 [ (k+1)^2+(2x+3)(k+1)]-[k^2+(2x+3)k]=2k+2x+4>0.
\]
No time horizon or orbit tableau is supplied.

For natural $x,t$, a rational root $k$ of the monic integer polynomial
$k^2+(2x+3)k-t$ is integral, so the same fiber statement holds over nonnegative
rationals. Over nonnegative reals the quadratic increases from zero to infinity;
every natural $t$, including non-hit times, has a root.

Evaluating $k(k+2x+3)-t$ and squaring uses four additions or subtractions and
two multiplications; at fixed gap the counts are two and two. These are
straight-line evaluator counts, excluding input construction and bit complexity.
The certificate is specific to this timing formula: mere decidability does
not imply a general fixed-arity single-fold quartic compiler.

\wtag\ [By Theorem \texttt{cdc:of:thm:classification} of Part~XI of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates} ($\mathrm D^+_2=\mathrm D^+_3=\mathrm{SL}$), the natural zero set of a polynomial of degree at most three that is nonnegative on the real nonnegative orthant has semilinear projections. The hit set $\{(x,t)\}$ of~\eqref{smc:fm:eq:quartic} is not semilinear (its sections are not eventually periodic, Proposition~\ref{smc:fm:prop:hits}), so it has no such representation of degree at most three, single-fold or not, and the degree four of~\eqref{smc:fm:eq:quartic} is the least possible in that class. The programme's review~\cite{reviewlowmass} confirms the uniqueness of the witness over the naturals and the nonnegative rationals, the real counterexample at $x=0$, $t=1$ (witness $(\sqrt{13}-3)/2$), the loss of uniqueness over signed integers, and the schedule of two multiplications and four additions.]

\section{Primary attribution and scope of the literature check}\label{smc:fm:sec:attribution}
Kong's June 2021 thesis~\cite{kongthesis} studies one-dimensional \emph{two-state}
NCCAs explicitly. Its Definition 1 is on printed p.~8 (PDF p.~9).
Section 2.4 proves translated eventual periodicity for two particles and
three-particle non-strong-universality; Theorem 2 appears on printed p.~14
(PDF p.~15). The following conclusion, printed p.~15 (PDF p.~16), describes
the four-particle case as open \emph{at that time}. These are direct primary
precursors. Their binary scope must not be silently enlarged to arbitrary
weighted alphabets. \wtag\ [Theorem~\ref{smc:fm:thm:main} covers two-state NCCAs, whose unique unit state is~1, for its decision problems, and so bears on the four-particle case that Kong's thesis called open in 2021, subject to the thesis's notion of strong universality, which is not compared here.]

Imai's final KAKEN report dated 25 June 2021~\cite{kakenfinal}, PDF p.~4,
reports a motion-representation decidability result at particle count at most
three. Its brief statement does not identify the exact decision problem.
Its methods discussion on PDF p.~3 distinguishes pebble-machine state from
numerical NCCA state that must be encoded geometrically, and discusses the
five-particle construction and smaller-particle analysis. The unspecified
word ``decidable'' should not be read as a sourced statement of uniform timed
Presburger definability or of the full weighted theorem here.

The Alhazov--Imai 2016 publisher abstract~\cite{alhazovimai} describes
finite-support nonnegative-integer-state NCCAs and reports universality with
five particles at sufficiently large finite radius. This is an upper bound,
not an abstract claim of minimality. Its full paper was unavailable in this
check. The thesis's nearby upper-bound reference appears mismatched, so the
2016 result is cited independently rather than inherited through that reference.

No later primary four-particle resolution was located in the targeted search.
This is not evidence that the 2021 question remains open today and is not
evidence of novelty of the present proof. The mathematical claim in this report
rests on its argument, not on a literature-status assertion.

\section{Checks and remaining questions}\label{smc:fm:sec:checks}
The general decision theorem is a mathematical proof, independently inspected;
there is no implemented rule-to-section compiler and no machine-checked
formalization. Finite examples cannot verify a theorem over all rules.
The explicit binary rule, by contrast, has a complete exported local table
and an exact finite conservation certificate.

The producer's reproducible standard-library Python checks include all 8192
identities in (\ref{smc:fm:eq:potential}), 262,144 exhaustive width-eighteen
conservation cases, 8,192 component-rule/local-table comparisons, 10,000 larger
random locality and translation comparisons, 302,400 direct trajectory steps,
2,424 predicted hits, and 20,000 finite unique-witness checks. A separately
written replay, importing no producer evaluator, checked all 8192 local outputs,
all 8192 potential identities, 49,640 direct steps over 40 initial gaps, and
840 exact pattern hits. A second independent guarded-pattern detector
reconstructed every local output, checked 1,396 complete closed-form transitions
and hit-exclusivity cases through section index $10^{80}$, and made 25
large-integer quartic/discriminant checks. The section arithmetic audit also
checked 951 first-contact instances and huge residue shifts, 272 weighted-shuttle
transitions, and 27,360 original-frame support bounds. These ledgers supplement the exact rule and timing
proofs; they do not establish the general theorem by testing.

The portable package contains this editable \LaTeX\ source and PDF, the full
rule table and potential, reproduction scripts, recorded test ledgers,
independent audit materials, and exact figure data. The earlier mass-three
release is not changed by this package.

\wtag\ \textbf{The release in this report.} These are the delivered names. The typed weighted shuttle whose 272 transitions are counted above is described in \path{17-four-mass-EXPANDING-SHUTTLE.md} and tested by \path{code/17-four-mass-test_expanding_shuttle.py}. The figure table \path{data/17-four-mass-binary-four-particle-shuttle.csv} keeps its delivered CRLF line ends. Not shipped: the manuscript, PDF, README, checksum ledger and the raster copy of the figure. \path{test_binary_expanding_shuttle.py} rewrites the sealed conservation certificate in place, with CRLF on Windows, after which \path{test_exact_boundaries.py} fails its hash test; run the suite on a copy and restore the certificate first (README). The programme's review reran all nine invocations and reproduced every receipt~\cite{reviewlowmass}.
