gives an eventual quadratic quasipolynomial. Equation~\eqref{smc:og:eq:Hgeneral} supplies its common leading coefficient. Solving each eventual residue polynomial inequality $H(M)\le T$ and taking the largest admissible $M$ gives the stated floor root description of $V$.
\end{proof}

\section{First arrival times and coverage}\label{smc:og:sec:arrival}
The spatial union alone does not tell us when a site first appears. The complete phase form supplies the missing timing information. Let $R(y,n,h)$ be the Presburger relation asserting that site $y$ is occupied at offset $h$ in the half open cycle $n$. The offset is affine in the phase parameters: preceding edge durations are affine in $n$, and a packet period contributes its fixed period length times $j$ plus a retained phase offset.

Exclude the finite set of sites first seen before $T_0$, treating those separately. Define $n_*(y)$ as the least cycle containing $y$, then $h_*(y)$ as the least offset in that cycle. Their graphs are Presburger: impose occurrence and negate any occurrence at an earlier cycle, or at an earlier offset in the same least cycle. Minimization is legitimate here because the cycle and time domains are nonnegative integers and every $y$ in the domain is eventually visited.

\wtag\ [The next lemma is classical: Presburger-definable functions have semilinear graphs~\cite{ginsburgspanier} and are therefore piecewise rational affine. Source~20 proves it directly; no novelty is claimed for it.]
\begin{lemma}[Presburger functions are piecewise rational affine]\label{smc:og:lem:affine}
A Presburger definable function with integer input and output is rational affine on each part of an effective finite semilinear partition of its domain.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Decompose its graph into finitely many linear sets. On one such component write the generators as $(v_i,w_i)$, with $v_i$ in the input coordinates and $w_i$ in the output coordinates. Every integer relation $\sum_i a_i v_i=0$ also satisfies $\sum_i a_i w_i=0$: separate positive and negative coefficients to obtain two points of this graph component with the same input, then use functionality. Therefore $v_i\mapsto w_i$ extends to a rational linear map on the rational span of the $v_i$. Adding the base point makes the function rational affine on the component's domain. A finite Boolean refinement of the semilinear domains yields a partition. All steps are effective.
\end{proof}
This argument allows lower dimensional input pieces and several output coordinates. It asserts no uniqueness of the semilinear parameter witnesses.

By the lemma, both $n_*(y)$ and $h_*(y)$ are piecewise rational affine. Refining their partitions jointly gives
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:firstarrival}
 \tau(y)=T_0+cn_*(y)^2+bn_*(y)+h_*(y)
\end{equation}
as a rational polynomial of degree at most two on every cell. Earlier prefix sites contribute singleton cells. This proves the exact effective statement in Theorem~\ref{smc:og:thm:timing}.

The source support bound $\inorm y\le K_0+K_1n$ implies that the earliest cycle of a distant site is at least a positive constant times $\inorm y$ minus a constant. Since section times are quadratic, $\tau(y)=\Omega(\inorm y^2)$. Conversely, finitely many affine formulas for $n_*(y)$ give $n_*(y)=O(1+\inorm y)$; the within cycle duration is $O(1+n)$. Hence $\tau(y)=O(1+\inorm y^2)$. This proves~\eqref{smc:og:eq:arrivalorder}.

To obtain box coverage time, the upper bound applies uniformly to sites of norm at most $N$. For the lower bound, choose fixed $0<\varepsilon<1$. Equation~\eqref{smc:og:eq:Cgeneral} gives $C(N)-C(\lfloor\varepsilon N\rfloor)>0$ for all sufficiently large $N$, so some visited site in the box has norm at least $\varepsilon N$. Its first arrival takes $\Omega(N^2)$ time.

\begin{remark}
Piecewise quadratic first arrival does not imply that the full space and time orbit is Presburger. The relation $t=cn^2+bn+T_0$ contains a genuine quadratic clock. A piecewise quadratic formula by itself is not yet a canonical Diophantine certificate. Appendix~\ref{smc:og:app:quartic} supplies the additional disjoint clause construction, signed quotient encoding, and uniqueness proof.
\end{remark}

\section{A globally conservative planar wedge automaton}\label{smc:og:sec:wedge}
We now give actual CAs realizing the geometric phenomena. Their unrestricted conservation is proved directly, rather than inferred from an abstract shuttle schedule or from finite simulations.

Work on $\Z^2$ with alphabet $\{0,u,E,W\}$ and weights
\[
 w(0)=0,\qquad w(u)=1,\qquad w(E)=w(W)=2.
\]
The letters $E,W$ denote internal head states. There is only one unit symbol. Let $e_1=(1,0)$, $e_2=(0,1)$. A head at $a$ is eligible if there is no other head within maximum norm distance four. Its action reads the old configuration. Any failed guard gives no rewrite. Every site not explicitly written stays unchanged.

\begin{table}[ht]
\centering\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.5}
\begin{tabular}{@{}c p{2.45in} p{2.65in}@{}}
\toprule
Rule & Required old symbols & New symbols at written sites\\
\midrule
1 & $E$ at $a$; $0$ at $a+e_1$ & $0$ at $a$; $E$ at $a+e_1$\\
2 & $E$ at $a$; $u$ at $a+e_1$; $0$ at $a+2e_1+e_2$ & $W$ at $a$; $0$ at $a+e_1$; $u$ at $a+2e_1+e_2$\\
3 & $W$ at $a$; $0$ at $a-e_1$ & $0$ at $a$; $W$ at $a-e_1$\\
4 & $W$ at $a$; $u$ at $a-e_1$; $0$ at $a+e_2$ and $a-e_1+e_2$ & $0$ at $a$ and $a-e_1$; $E$ at $a+e_2$; $u$ at $a-e_1+e_2$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{The complete planar rewrite rule, in addition to the head isolation condition. All vacancy tests are part of the definition.}
\label{smc:og:tab:rule}
\end{table}

\begin{proposition}[Full rule validity]\label{smc:og:prop:rule}
Simultaneously applying Table~\ref{smc:og:tab:rule} at eligible heads defines a translation equivariant radius six CA. It preserves total weight on every finite configuration, including configurations with many heads, obstructed destinations, and extra unit symbols. Its vacuum is quiescent and its isolated unit is stationary.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Rules 1 and 2 have mutually exclusive forward guards; so do rules 3 and 4. All writes lie in $a+[-2,2]^2$. Distinct eligible heads are more than four apart, so their write cubes are disjoint. No eligible rewrite can overwrite an ineligible head: a head at a written site would be within distance two and would itself disqualify the candidate. Each listed rewrite preserves the exact weight sum on its written sites. Every newly filled site is explicitly required to be vacant unless it is the head's own rewritten site. In particular no additional unit is silently erased.

For locality, the output at $x$ only needs to consider heads within distance two of $x$ and their radius four isolation tests. All this lies within distance six of $x$. The listed destination guards are within the same radius six neighborhood. If no action writes $x$, retain its old value. This is a well defined finite radius local rule. Disjoint, weight preserving rewrites prove conservation for all finite inputs. Vacuum and an isolated unit have no eligible head action and remain unchanged.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Sections and the exact spatial wedge}
Fix $k\ge3$. Start with units at $(0,0)$ and $(k,0)$ and an $E$ head at $(1,0)$. There is exactly one head forever, so its eligibility test always passes. Inductively the section at cycle $n$ is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:wedge-section}
 L_n=(0,n),\qquad R_n=(k+n,n),\qquad E\text{ at }(1,n).
\end{equation}
Put $K=k+n$. There are $K-2$ eastward free steps, one right bounce by rule 2, $K-2$ westward free steps, and one left bounce by rule 4. The duration is $2(K-1)$, so
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:wedge-time}
 T_n=n^2+(2k-3)n.
\end{equation}
The right bounce moves the right unit to $(k+n+1,n+1)$. The left bounce moves the left unit and head up by one, yielding the next section. All guards hold along this intended orbit.

Every row $n$ eventually has exactly the visited horizontal coordinates $0,1,\ldots,k+n$. The early arrival of the right unit on row $n+1$ adds only that next row's endpoint. Therefore the entire occupied site union is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:wedge-set}
 S=\{(x,y)\in\Z^2:y\ge0,\ 0\le x\le k+y\}.
\end{equation}
This realizes $E=e_2$, $\nu=e_1$, $\Delta=1$, $c=1$, and rank two. No special rare label or section subsequence is being counted.

For $N\ge k$, direct summation gives
\begin{align}
 C(N)&=\sum_{y=0}^N\bigl(\min(N,k+y)+1\bigr)\notag\\
 &=\frac{N^2+(2k+3)N+2+k-k^2}{2}.\label{smc:og:eq:wedge-count}
\end{align}
Thus $\kappa=1/2$ and the density in centered square boxes is $1/8$. A finite union of bounded width lines has only $O(N)$ lattice sites there, so rank two is sharp even against finitely many lines. The wedge does not explore a half plane.

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.92\linewidth]{figures/20-orbit-geometry-visited-geometry.pdf}
\caption{Actual occupied site unions from the authored local rule simulator, with $k=3$. Left: the stationary frame wedge through section $n=15$. Right: the same planar rule with a vertical shift after every step, through time $180$. Colors distinguish the persistent left marker, head, and right marker in this intended one head orbit; the displayed sets are not an abstract flight schedule.}
\label{smc:og:fig:geometry}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Exact discovery and first arrival for this example}
At the section time $T_n$, the number of sites already visited is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:Bn}
 B_n=\frac{n(n-1)}2+(k+1)n+3.
\end{equation}
Rows below $n$ are complete and row $n$ contributes its two left positions and right endpoint. For $0\le h<2(k+n-1)$,
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:wedge-V}
 V(T_n+h)=B_n+\min(h,k+n-2)+\mathbf1_{\{h\ge k+n-1\}}.
\end{equation}
The eastward flight discovers its interior one site per step. The right bounce discovers one next row endpoint. The westward flight revisits old sites, and the final left bounce creates two sites counted at the next section. Formula~\eqref{smc:og:eq:wedge-V} exposes the within cycle oscillation hidden in $V(T)=T/2+O(\sqrt T)$.

For row $n\ge1$, first arrival is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:wedge-arrival}
 \tau(x,n)=
 \begin{cases}
 T_n,&x=0\text{ or }1,\\
 T_n+x-1,&2\le x\le k+n-1,\\
 T_n-(k+n-2),&x=k+n.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
The last line is the preceding cycle's right bounce. On row zero, the three initial sites have time zero and each other $2\le x\le k-1$ has time $x-1$. These formulas show explicitly the finite semilinear partition and rational quadratic first arrival description.

\section{Rank one behavior and nonsemilinear drift}\label{smc:og:sec:rankone}
Degree zero occurs for one stationary unit. Degree one occurs for an isolated $E$ head moving east under the preceding rule. These inputs have mass one and two, respectively.

For an expanding rank one example, change the planar rule in exactly two places:
\begin{enumerate}
\item In rule 2, change both the vacant destination guard and the new unit destination from $a+2e_1+e_2$ to $a+2e_1$.
\item Replace rule 4 by: if $W$ is at $a$ and $u$ is at $a-e_1$, change the head in place to $E$ and leave that neighboring unit unchanged. Remove the former upward destination vacancy tests, since no new site is filled in this action.
\end{enumerate}
The isolation convention and rules 1 and 3 are unchanged. This is a complete guard and write specification. The same disjoint rewrite proof establishes a radius six globally conservative CA.

From the same input, sections become $L_n=(0,0)$, $R_n=(k+n,0)$, with $E$ at $(1,0)$, and retain clock~\eqref{smc:og:eq:wedge-time}. Its full visited set is the nonnegative horizontal ray. The exact time count, after the initial interior has been visited, is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:rankone-V}
 V(T)=\left\lfloor\sqrt{T+(k-1)(k-2)}\right\rfloor+3,
 \qquad T\ge k-2.
\end{equation}
Indeed site $x\ge k+1$ is first reached by the right marker at time
\[
 (x-2)^2-(k-1)(k-2).
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:rankone-H}
 H(M)=(M-3)^2-(k-1)(k-2),\qquad M\ge k+2.
\end{equation}
This directly realizes the rank one inverse theorem, with $\alpha=c=1$. It also proves that an actual permitted CA can have nonquasipolynomial $V(T)$: an unbounded eventual quasipolynomial cannot grow like $\sqrt T$.

Now call this horizontal rule $G$ and set $F=\tau_{e_2}G$. Translation commutes with $G$, so the isolated unit drift is $e_2$ and normalization recovers $G$. The full original frame occupied site set is
\[
 S_F=\{(x,t):x\in\supp(G^t(c_0)),\ t\ge0\},
\]
where the horizontal support is read as a set of $x$ coordinates. Every row contains exactly three sites; all satisfy $x\ge0$ and $x=O(\sqrt t)$, yet $x$ is unbounded.

\begin{proposition}\label{smc:og:prop:nonsemilinear}
The full set $S_F$ in this example is not semilinear.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Suppose it is a finite union of linear sets, and let $(a,b)$ be a period vector of a nonempty component. Negative $b$ is impossible because arbitrarily large period multiples would enter negative rows. If $b=0$, finite rows force $a=0$. If $b>0$, the uniform sublinear bound on $x$ forces $a=0$: otherwise along its multiples $|x|/t$ tends to the nonzero ratio $|a|/b$. Hence every period is vertical, and finitely many component base points bound $x$ globally, a contradiction.
\end{proof}
This proof concerns every occupied site, not just special labels or sparse section times. Its nonsemilinearity alone does not rule out an eventual polynomial box count. The next example supplies that separate conclusion.

\section{An exact nonquasipolynomial box count}\label{smc:og:sec:driftcount}
Return to the planar wedge rule of Section~\ref{smc:og:sec:wedge} and again set $F=\tau_{e_2}G$, now shifting inside the wedge's plane. At time $t$ in cycle $n$, let $\sigma(t)=0$ before that cycle's right bounce finishes, and $1$ afterward. The heights of the three objects in the original frame are
\[
 \text{left: }t+n,\qquad \text{head: }t+n,\qquad
 \text{right: }t+n+\sigma(t).
\]
Every trajectory has strictly increasing height. Usually height increases by one; at its one upward move in each cycle it increases by two. The left marker and head have the same height sequence and different horizontal coordinates.

There are no cross time overlaps with the right trajectory. Before the right bounce it has the head's height at the same time but strictly larger $x$. After the bounce its height is either the head's next height or, at the left bounce, a missing head height. In the former case the right coordinate is $k+n+1$, while the head's next coordinate is at most $k+n-1$. The three entire trajectories are therefore pairwise disjoint, not merely distinct at each instant.

The left marker and head skip the heights
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:holesA}
 a_n=T_n+n-1=n^2+(2k-2)n-1,\qquad n\ge1.
\end{equation}
The right bounce finishes at $t=T_n+k+n-1$, so the right trajectory skips
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:holesB}
 b_n=T_n+k+2n-1=n^2+(2k-1)n+k-1,\qquad n\ge0.
\end{equation}
Each height sequence starts at zero and contains exactly the nonnegative integers outside its displayed skipped sequence. The number of its holes through height $N$ is thus a quadratic inequality count.

For $N\ge2k+4$, the horizontal box constraint is inactive at all heights at most $N$. One way to see this is that the first right marker occurrence at coordinate $k+j$, $j\ge1$, has height
\[
 (j-1)^2+(2k-1)(j-1)+k.
\]
If its horizontal coordinate exceeds $N\ge k+1$, this height exceeds $N$ as well. The head is never farther right than the marker. We retain the convenient conservative threshold $2k+4$ in the formula and audits.

Define
\begin{align}
 A(N)&=\max\left(0,\left\lfloor\sqrt{(k-1)^2+N+1}\right\rfloor-(k-1)\right),\label{smc:og:eq:AN}\\
 B(N)&=\max\left(0,1+\left\lfloor
 \frac{-(2k-1)+\sqrt{(2k-1)^2+4(N-k+1)}}2
 \right\rfloor\right).\label{smc:og:eq:BN}
\end{align}
These count~\eqref{smc:og:eq:holesA} and~\eqref{smc:og:eq:holesB}, respectively, with their different starting indices.

\begin{theorem}[Exact drifted wedge count]\label{smc:og:thm:exactdrift}
For every integer $k\ge3$ and $N\ge2k+4$, the original frame full occupied site count is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:exactdrift}
 C_F(N)=3(N+1)-2A(N)-B(N).
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:driftasym}
 C_F(N)=3N-3\sqrt N+O(1),
\end{equation}
and $C_F$ is not eventually quasipolynomial. Thus $S_F$ is not semilinear either.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The three trajectories are disjoint, and their heights are precisely the nonnegative integers minus the displayed holes. For $N$ in the stated range, counting their points in the box is exactly counting these height sequences through $N$. This gives~\eqref{smc:og:eq:exactdrift}. Both $A(N)$ and $B(N)$ equal $\sqrt N+O(1)$, proving~\eqref{smc:og:eq:driftasym}.

If $C_F$ were eventually quasipolynomial, its linear growth and $C_F(N)/N\to3$ would force every residue polynomial to be $3N$ plus a constant. Then $C_F(N)-3N$ would be bounded, contradicting the negative square root term. A semilinear $S_F$ would have an eventual quasipolynomial box count by the already cited Presburger theorem, so it too is excluded.
\end{proof}
Formula~\eqref{smc:og:eq:exactdrift} is the exact statement. Its bounded floor oscillations are retained; equation~\eqref{smc:og:eq:driftasym} is not a floor free expansion to all orders. The inverse box radius satisfies the useful two term estimate
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:driftinverse}
 \min\{N:C_F(N)\ge M\}=M/3+\sqrt{M/3}+O(1).
\end{equation}
Substitute $N=M/3+\sqrt{M/3}+L$ into~\eqref{smc:og:eq:driftasym}; a fixed sufficiently large positive or negative $L$ brackets the level $M$. Exact inversion remains governed by the floor formula and monotonicity.

\section{General drift bounds and rank three sharpness}\label{smc:og:sec:rankthree}
For any expanding shuttle, add $\delta$ times its actual time to~\eqref{smc:og:eq:phase}. Actual time is $T_n$ plus an affine function of $n,j$. Every resulting point therefore lies in a fixed affine translate of
\[
 \operatorname{span}_{\R}\{E,\nu,\delta\}.
\]
There are only finitely many phase offsets. The quadratic degree in $n$ is the source of the extra geometry. Drift restoration is not an affine change of the untimed spatial parameters alone.

In independent and translated periodic tails, actual time is affine in the common period index. Drift restoration simply changes each ray vector, preserving semilinearity.

\subsection{Every nonzero drifting shuttle has linear box order}
The stationary support norm bound and quadratic clock imply, uniformly over the at most four occupied sites at time $t$,
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:driftsupport}
 y_F(t)=t\delta+O(\sqrt t).
\end{equation}
If $\delta\ne0$, any original frame site in the radius $N$ box must occur at $t=O(N)$. There are at most four occupied sites at each time, so $C_F(N)=O(N)$.

For the matching lower bound, choose fixed small $\eta>0$ and $T=\lfloor\eta N\rfloor$, so all support through $T$ lies in the radius $N$ box once $N$ is large. Pick an occupied site at time $T$. Quiescence and finite radius imply that it has an occupied predecessor within a fixed radius at time $T-1$; otherwise its entire input neighborhood would be vacuum. Repeat to time zero. This produces a bounded jump path of occupied sites contained in the box. A coordinate with $\delta_i\ne0$ has endpoint displacement $\Theta(N)$ by~\eqref{smc:og:eq:driftsupport}. A bounded jump path spanning that displacement contains $\Omega(N)$ distinct sites. Thus $C_F(N)=\Theta(N)$ without assuming labeled particle identities or mistaking occurrences for distinct sites.

\subsection{A three dimensional realization}
Embed the planar wedge rule in $\Z^3$ using its $e_1,e_2$ directions and the same radius four head isolation test in the three dimensional maximum norm. The disjoint write and radius six proof is unchanged. Compose with the shift $\delta=e_3$.

At section times the original frame support contains
\[
 (0,n,T_n),\qquad (k+n,n,T_n).
\]
If all visited sites stayed within bounded distance of one affine plane of rank at most two, some nonzero real linear functional
\[
 \ell(x,y,z)=ax+by+cz
\]
would stay bounded on them. Its value on the first sequence is $bn+c[n^2+(2k-3)n]$, so boundedness forces $b=c=0$. Its value on the second then forces $a=0$, a contradiction.

The stronger finite union assertion requires the flights, rather than only those two section sequences. The eastward flight contains
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:flightgrid}
 (1+j,n,T_n+j),\qquad 0\le j\le k+n-2.
\end{equation}
On these points,
\[
 \ell=c n^2+\bigl(b+c(2k-3)\bigr)n+(a+c)j+a.
\]
If $a+c\ne0$, a fixed bounded strip for $\ell$ contains only $O(1)$ of the integer $j$ values in each cycle. If $a+c=0$, this is a nonconstant unbounded polynomial in $n$ unless $a=b=c=0$, so the strip contains none of the flight for every sufficiently large $n$. A finite collection of strips therefore covers only $O(1)$ of its $\Theta(n)$ sites. Every bounded width affine plane of rank at most two lies inside one such strip.

\begin{corollary}\label{smc:og:cor:rankthree}
The rank three upper bound is sharp even if finitely many bounded width affine planes of rank at most two are allowed, with arbitrary orientations.
\end{corollary}
The original frame example still has only linear centered box order. Containing rank, growth degree, and semilinearity are distinct properties.

\section{Prior work and mathematical interpretation}\label{smc:og:sec:literature}
The geometric picture is close to classical two pebble automata. Lacalle and Gajardo~\cite{lacalle2014}, printed pages 2 and 5, describe conical confinement, zigzag coverage, and expanding oscillations versus periodic behavior, attributing core predecessors to Delorme and Mazoyer. Their conclusion on printed page 17 extends their geometric arguments to $\Z^d$. Their principal theorem concerns real time trace recognition, a different statement from the present fixed input site enumeration and weighted CA classification.

Blum and Sakoda~\cite{blum1977}, printed page 148, discuss sector exploration below a half plane and the inability of one finite automaton with two pebbles to explore an entire half plane. The pointed cone statement here should be read alongside that close predecessor geometry. Delorme and Mazoyer~\cite{delorme2002} remains a central reference. Its publisher abstract and the explicit account in Lacalle and Gajardo were checked; the complete 2002 text was not retrieved for this report. That limitation supplies no basis for a priority inference.

The explicit CAs use a mass two stateful head and two identical mass one markers. Report 29's \textup{[source~19's]} classification also covers splitting, fusion, weighted symbols, and independent packet tails. Its proof is the required bridge; a general sparse CA should not silently be identified with a pebble machine. Conversely, the semilinearity to counting implication is existing Presburger mathematics~\cite{woods2015}.

For Diophantine computational substrates, the useful distinction is between untimed spatial definability and the extra quadratic clock. The spatial set admits effective linear arithmetic descriptions and rational generating functions, while first arrival and inverse discovery can be genuinely quadratic. Drift couples that clock into spatial coordinates, creating nonsemilinear sets and square root corrections despite a very small finite conserved mass. This is a concrete separation of descriptive resources, not an undecidability or universality claim.

\subsection{What remains open here}
\begin{enumerate}
\item\label{smc:og:q:binary} Determine which of these sharp geometric and drift phenomena admit binary or reversible realizations under the same small mass restrictions. The weighted examples do not answer this. (\wtag\ Added 3 October 2026: answered for binary rules by source~21, whose ordinary binary radius-six rule realizes a planar wedge, with two missing lines, and the drifted square-root regime (Theorems~\ref{smc:bp:thm:visited} and~\ref{smc:bp:thm:Fcount}). Reversible realizations stay open: source~21's rules are not asserted reversible.)
\item\label{smc:og:q:algorithms} Extract practical algorithms and size bounds for the effective semilinear partitions, counting chambers, and first arrival formulas. General effectivity alone need not be computationally useful.
\item\label{smc:og:q:quartic} Optimize the canonical first arrival Diophantine encoding of Appendix~\ref{smc:og:app:quartic}, especially the cell count, quotient variables, and shared quadratic lifts, without mistaking input dependent arity for a uniform bound. (\wtag\ Open; source~21's 614-auxiliary quartic certifies one local transition of its binary rule, not a first-arrival relation.)
\item\label{smc:og:q:subleading} Classify possible subleading terms and exact inverse formulas in the original frame. The present example exhibits a square root correction; the general $\Theta(N)$ theorem does not assert an exhaustive asymptotic taxonomy.
\item\label{smc:og:q:pebble} Clarify the exact correspondence with each classical pebble model, including indistinguishable markers, collision semantics, and its chosen observation convention, before making any novelty comparison.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Reproducibility and proof boundaries}\label{smc:og:sec:reproduction}
The portable release contains this editable source and PDF, authored figures with their simulator, the proof and independent counting review, finite scientific checkers, expected JSON receipts, and a sealed replay entry point. No external paper cache is included. Bibliographic links are sufficient to locate the primary literature.

The main checker implements both explicit rule variants, the section clock, stationary box formula, and exact drifted count. It also samples malformed configurations and compares global rewrites with cellwise radius six evaluation. That cellwise strategy reuses the same action predicate: it is an independently evaluated local output route, not an independently reimplemented transition rule. A separate included example checker independently transcribes the transition rules and checks first arrival and boundary arithmetic.

Every scientific correctness check uses explicit exceptions rather than removable Python assertions. A deliberately failing guard self test confirms enforcement under optimization. The replay runs fresh isolated normal and optimized copies, compares receipts recursively by exact type and value, and confirms that packaged file bytes, modes, and modification times remain unchanged. Booleans cannot masquerade as integer counts. An independently fixed inventory and pins are checked before invoking any packaged helper; a manifest by itself is insufficient when edited together with its payload.

The bundled tamper regressions test ordinary payload changes, removal paired with manifest editing, coordinated checker and manifest alteration, a type only receipt mutation, an extra file, and a symlink payload. Their aim is narrowly to exercise the stated verification boundary. This is not an adversarial security system against an attacker who replaces the trusted bootstrap and its reference pins together.

A PDF build writes only to an external directory. The release is not modified by replay or rebuild. Rebuilding requires a conventional \LaTeX{} distribution; the geometry and example checkers use the Python standard library, while the optional first visit algebra fixtures use SymPy. Figure regeneration additionally uses Matplotlib. All pages of the final PDF are rendered and visually inspected for clipping, overlap, missing symbols, and reference errors.

Finite checks audit transcription, formula arithmetic, and selected boundary cases. They do not establish conservation on all configurations, classification termination, semilinearity, or asymptotic claims. Those conclusions rest on the unrestricted mathematical arguments in this report and the precisely stated Report 29 \textup{[source~19]} dependency. No binary CA construction, reversibility proof, formal proof assistant certification, efficient general compiler, or new universality threshold is claimed.

\wtag\ \textbf{The release in this report.} These are the delivered names. \path{build.sh}, \path{reproduce.sh}, \path{replay.py}, \path{tamper_regression.py}, \path{figures/make_figures.py} and the checkers \path{scientific/geometry/audit.py}, \path{scientific/examples/independent-audit.py} and \path{scientific/first-visit/audit.py} are shipped as \path{code/20-orbit-geometry-*} (sub-directories joined to the name by hyphens, for example \path{code/20-orbit-geometry-geometry-audit.py}); the normal and optimized receipts of the geometry and first-visit checkers and the four \path{verification/*.json} records as \path{data/20-orbit-geometry-*}; the figure as \path{figures/20-orbit-geometry-visited-geometry.pdf}; and the nine proof, review and supplement notes of \path{scientific/} as \path{20-orbit-geometry-*.md} at the report root. Not shipped: the manuscript (printed here), its PDF, README and checksum ledger, and the two supplement checksum manifests \path{scientific/first-visit/MANIFEST.sha256} and \path{scientific/geometry/MANIFEST.sha256}. The replay entry point needs the delivered layout (README). At placement on Windows, \path{replay.py} passed in normal and optimized mode and \path{tamper_regression.py} rejected every tamper case.


\bigskip
\phantomsection\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Source 21: A binary planar four particle shuttle}
\noindent{\large\bfseries Source 21 (batch-82 manuscript 01): A binary planar four particle shuttle}\par
\noindent\emph{Exact orbit geometry and a one step quartic certificate}, ``Research report 31'', 3 October 2026. \wtag\ From the archive \path{Binary_Planar_Four_Particle_Shuttle_Package.zip}; its labels carry the prefix \texttt{smc:bp:}, and its sections are Sections~\ref{smc:bp:sec:scope}--\ref{smc:bp:sec:limits} here. It builds on sources~17, 18 and~20 (``Reports~12, 13 and~30''), which it pins by SHA-256, and modifies source~17's binary shuttle; no code is shared. The Hilbert's-tenth programme has reviewed it (Section~\ref{smc:bp:sec:verification}). Its abstract follows; the bracketed notes in it are \wtag.
\begin{quote}\small
We give an ordinary binary, quiescent, conservative cellular automaton on $\Z^2$ with an explicit four-particle expanding shuttle. Its stationary-frame visited set is a wedge with exactly two missing lines, its centered-box count has leading coefficient $1/2$, and its first-arrival times and generalized inverse are explicit. Composing the rule with unit vertical translation gives four disjoint traces and the exact floor-root count $4N-4\sqrt N+O_k(1)$, with inverse $M/4+\sqrt{M/4}+O_k(1)$. Both specified rules have exact centered Chebyshev radius six and precisely 78 essential input cells. Their unique multilinear Boolean polynomial has degree 45. A separately constructed one-step sum-of-squares quartic has 614 canonical natural auxiliaries, 693 residuals, 79 external variables, and exactly 3403 collected terms. Its auxiliary tuple is unique even over the reals. The construction modifies the two bounce outputs of the one-dimensional binary shuttle in Report 12 [source~17] and retains its moving pairs and quadratic clock. It answers the binary-realization question left by Report 30's weighted planar example [more precisely: it gives a binary realization of the planar wedge and of the drifted square-root regime of source~20's weighted example, answering source~20's first question for binary rules but not for reversible ones]. Full-shift correctness is proved, rather than inferred from finite tests. No reversibility, universality, optimal-radius, or general finite-fold Diophantine claim is made.
\end{quote}

\section{Scope and relation to the earlier reports}\label{smc:bp:sec:scope}
Report 30 established quantitative geometry for promised conservative cellular automata of fixed mass at most four and supplied a weighted planar realization~\textup{[source~20, Part~\ref{smc:zd:part}]}. The present report supplies a binary realization directly. Every occupied site has weight one, and the selected orbit contains exactly four occupied sites at every time. The proof of this example is self-contained and does not require a general orbit-classification theorem.
\wtag\ [Its counts are exact instances of source~20's Theorems~\ref{smc:og:thm:geometry} and~\ref{smc:og:thm:timing} (an expanding shuttle of rank two, with leading coefficient $1/2$) and, after the vertical shift, of Theorem~\ref{smc:og:thm:drift} (linear box order with a square-root deficit, as in source~20's Theorem~\ref{smc:og:thm:exactdrift}).]

The starting mechanism is the one-dimensional binary shuttle in Report 12, Section 7 \textup{[Section~\ref{smc:fm:sec:binary} here, rule~\eqref{smc:fm:eq:binary}]}, ``An explicit binary expanding shuttle''~\textup{[source~17, Part~\ref{smc:su:part}]}. Its replacements, up to translation, are
\[
\{0,1\}\mapsto\{1,2\},\quad
\{0,2\}\mapsto\{-1,1\},\quad
\{0,1,3\}\mapsto\{-1,1,4\},\quad
\{0,2,4\}\mapsto\{0,3,4\}.
\]
We keep both moving-pair replacements and all four input shapes. At the right bounce, only the outgoing right marker moves up one row. At the left bounce, the entire output triple moves up one row. These two transverse changes make successive sweeps occupy successive rows. Thus the four horizontal patterns are not presented as new patterns. The quadratic section clock is inherited from the same flight lengths; the planar visited set and vertical-drift geometry are proved below.

Report 13 distinguishes local or fixed-formula certificates from more general timed constructions~\textup{[source~18, Part~\ref{smc:su:part}]}. We preserve that distinction. The quartic in Section~\ref{smc:bp:sec:quartic} describes \emph{one local transition}, with a literal expanded polynomial and exact resource ledger. It is not an unbounded orbit tableau. Report 30's \textup{[source~20's Corollary~\ref{smc:og:cor:original-target}]} separate complete-target theorem is discussed only in Section~\ref{smc:bp:sec:limits}; it does not follow from the local quartic.

Throughout, $k\ge7$ is an integer, $\NN=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$, and a ``natural witness'' means a tuple of nonnegative integers. The notation $O_k(1)$ permits a bound depending on the fixed initial gap $k$. Spatial counts count distinct lattice sites ever occupied, not visits, particles with multiplicity, or space-time events.

\section{The exact component rule}\label{smc:bp:sec:rule}
Identify a binary configuration with its occupied set $S\subseteq\Z^2$, which may be infinite. Join distinct occupied sites whenever their Chebyshev distance is at most two. A connected component that is a translate of an input $A$ in Table~\ref{smc:bp:tab:rule} is replaced by the corresponding translate of $B$. All other components remain unchanged. Only translates are recognized; neither reflections nor rotations are implicitly included. Call the resulting map $G$.

\begin{table}[htbp]\centering
\begin{tabular}{cll}\toprule
Rule & Input $A$ & Output $B$\\\midrule
$E$ & $\{(0,0),(1,0)\}$ & $\{(1,0),(2,0)\}$\\
$W$ & $\{(0,0),(2,0)\}$ & $\{(-1,0),(1,0)\}$\\
$R$ & $\{(0,0),(1,0),(3,0)\}$ & $\{(-1,0),(1,0),(4,1)\}$\\
$L$ & $\{(0,0),(2,0),(4,0)\}$ & $\{(0,1),(3,1),(4,1)\}$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{The four exact component replacements. A surrounding empty halo is part of recognition, not an optional condition.}\label{smc:bp:tab:rule}
\end{table}

\begin{lemma}[Recognition and bounded matching]\label{smc:bp:lem:matching}
The component prescription is unambiguous. On every finite or infinite configuration it gives a bijection from old occupied sites to new occupied sites whose displacements have max norm at most one. In particular, outputs from different components do not overlap.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Each input shape is connected in the distance-two graph. An exact translate has a unique anchor, namely its minimum $x$ coordinate and common $y$ coordinate. The pair shapes have different gaps; the triple shapes have different gap lists. Therefore distinct rules cannot recognize the same component. Isolated sites, nonhorizontal components, larger components, and infinite components are unrecognized and stay fixed.

Use the following source-to-target bijections within recognized components:
\begin{align*}
E:&\quad(0,0)\mapsto(1,0),\quad(1,0)\mapsto(2,0),\\
W:&\quad(0,0)\mapsto(-1,0),\quad(2,0)\mapsto(1,0),\\
R:&\quad(0,0)\mapsto(-1,0),\quad(1,0)\mapsto(1,0),\quad(3,0)\mapsto(4,1),\\
L:&\quad(0,0)\mapsto(0,1),\quad(2,0)\mapsto(3,1),\quad(4,0)\mapsto(4,1).
\end{align*}
Use the identity on every frozen component. Every displacement has norm at most one. Distinct components have all mutual distances at least three. If matched outputs from two components coincided, their source sites would have distance at most two by the triangle inequality, a contradiction. This includes a recognized component next to a frozen finite or infinite component. Outputs within each component are distinct by the displayed bijection. The combined matching is therefore a bijection onto the new occupied set.
\end{proof}

For finite $S$, the lemma proves exact particle conservation. For infinite $S$, it proves a well-defined bounded-displacement matching without subtracting infinite cardinalities. Two output components may join after a step; the next step simply recognizes or freezes the newly formed component. Such joining does not invalidate the one-step argument.

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=.99\linewidth]{figures/21-planar-shuttle-rule.pdf}
\caption{Exact primitive replacements and the source-to-target matching. Gray grid points are unoccupied reference sites. The two transverse bounce changes are visible in $R$ and $L$. The required empty radius-two halo around each input is not drawn.}\label{smc:bp:fig:rule}
\end{figure}

\section{Locality on the full shift}\label{smc:bp:sec:locality}
For a translated input $A+a$, define its halo and recognition predicate by
\[
H(A+a)=(A+a)+[-2,2]^2,\qquad
P_{A,a}(S)\Longleftrightarrow S\cap H(A+a)=A+a,
\]
where intervals denote integer lattice intervals. This finite predicate is equivalent to saying that $A+a$ is exactly an occupied connected component. Indeed, the shape is connected, and the first site of any occupied path leaving it must lie in its halo. Conversely, an empty surrounding halo excludes every such path. This works on infinite configurations as well: a fragment of a larger or infinite component cannot be falsely rewritten because a window truncates the rest.

To evaluate the new bit at $z$, consider only anchors for which $z$ is in a primitive input or output. If the old bit is zero, a successful predicate turns it on exactly when the corresponding replacement creates $z$. If the old bit is one, a successful predicate turns it off exactly when the replacement removes $z$. Otherwise the bit is preserved. Lemma~\ref{smc:bp:lem:matching} ensures that these local decisions agree with the component prescription on the entire binary full shift.

For every candidate and each input site $p$, one has $\inorm{p-z}\le4$. Its halo is therefore within radius six of $z$. More precisely, the changed-site cylinders read only
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:rectG}
D_G=[-6,6]\times[-3,2],\qquad |D_G|=78.
\end{equation}
The central unchanged bit lies in the same rectangle. All formulas commute with translations. No input shape is recognized in the vacuum, so $G$ is quiescent. This proves the upper radius bound on all $2^{169}$ square radius-six windows without enumerating them. Section~\ref{smc:bp:sec:degree} proves that the smaller 78-cell dependency rectangle is exact.

Let $\tau$ be vertical translation by $(0,1)$, with $\tau S=S+(0,1)$, and set
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:F}
F=\tau\circ G.
\end{equation}
Translation equivariance gives $F^t(S)=\tau^tG^t(S)$. Evaluating $F$ at $z$ evaluates $G$ at $z-(0,1)$, so its read rectangle is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:rectF}
D_F=[-6,6]\times[-4,1].
\end{equation}
Thus $F$ also has radius at most six, fixes the vacuum, and preserves every finite mass. Although the component matching for $G$ moves sites by at most one, no such displacement bound is needed to reprove conservation after the global translation.

\section{Four particles and the exact section clock}\label{smc:bp:sec:orbit}
Start from
\[
S_0=\{(0,0),(3,0),(4,0),(k,0)\},\qquad k\ge7.
\]
Put
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:clock}
T_n=n^2+(2k-11)n\qquad(n\in\NN).
\end{equation}
\begin{theorem}[Complete phase description]\label{smc:bp:thm:orbit}
For every $n\ge0$,
\[
G^{T_n}(S_0)=\{(0,n),(3,n),(4,n),(k+n,n)\}.
\]
Writing $d=k+n$, the cycle $[T_n,T_{n+1})$ consists of these two inclusive phase lists:
\begin{align}
G^{T_n+j}(S_0)&=\{(0,n),(3+j,n),(4+j,n),(d,n)\},
&&0\le j\le d-6,\label{smc:bp:eq:ephase}\\
G^{T_n+d-5+q}(S_0)&=\{(0,n),(d-4-q,n),(d-2-q,n),(d+1,n+1)\},
&&0\le q\le d-6.\label{smc:bp:eq:wphase}
\end{align}
In particular, each displayed support contains four distinct particles.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
At the section, the adjacent pair is isolated from both markers. For $j<d-6$, its separation from each marker is at least three, so $E$ advances it by one. At $j=d-6$, the right component is exactly the $R$ triple $\{d-3,d-2,d\}$ in row $n$; the left marker is separated because $d-3\ge4$. The next state is the $q=0$ state of~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:wphase}.

The returning gap-two pair is separated from the lifted marker by three columns: $(d+1)-(d-2)=3$. A row difference of one does not reduce this max-norm distance. The separation only grows during the return. For $q<d-6$, the pair's leftmost site is at least three, so $W$ applies and decreases both horizontal coordinates by one. At $q=d-6$, the lower component is the exact $L$ triple $\{0,2,4\}$, separated from the upper marker by at least $d-3\ge4$. One $L$ step gives the next section.

The duration is $(d-6)+1+(d-6)+1=2d-10$. Summing these durations for $d=k,k+1,\ldots,k+n-1$ gives~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:clock}. This proves induction from $n=0$ \textup{[read: this completes the induction on $n$]}. The endpoints in the two lists cover exactly $2d-10$ states and exclude the next section; they remain valid for $k=7$.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ [Theorem~\ref{smc:bp:thm:orbit} is the planar lift of source~17's Proposition~\ref{smc:fm:prop:hits}: the four input shapes, the two moving pairs, the cycle of $2d-10$ steps and the clock are source~17's, and so are the steps of the proof; only the transverse lifts of the right marker and of the left-bounce output are new, as source~21 says above.]

\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering
\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/21-planar-shuttle-orbits.pdf}
\caption{Actual simulation of the component rule from $k=7$. Left: the first eight completed rows visited by $G$, simulated through $T_8$, with the two missing families marked by open circles. Right: every occupied site of $F$ through time 96, colored by the four particles using the bounded matching. The drifted traces have no repeated site. Both panels are generated from the rule, not drawn from the count formulas.}\label{smc:bp:fig:orbits}
\end{figure}

\section{Visited sites and first arrivals}\label{smc:bp:sec:visits}
\begin{theorem}[The wedge and its two missing lines]\label{smc:bp:thm:visited}
The complete stationary-frame visited set is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:visited}
V_k=\{(0,n):n\ge0\}\ \cup\ \{(x,n):n\ge0,\ 2\le x\le k+n-2\}\ \cup\ \{(k+n,n):n\ge0\}.
\end{equation}
It is the integer wedge $n\ge0$, $0\le x\le k+n$ with precisely the two families $x=1$ and $x=k+n-1$ removed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
On row $n$, the east phase visits $[3,k+n-2]$ and the west phase additionally reaches $2$. It never reaches $1$. The markers occupy $0$ and $k+n$. The right marker may first occupy its row during the preceding cycle, but no extra sites result. Future cycles use higher rows. Hence the union is exactly~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:visited}.
\end{proof}

Let $\theta_k(x,n)$ be the first time $G^t(S_0)$ occupies $(x,n)$, and leave it undefined at an unvisited site. For $n\ge1$, put $d=k+n$. Then the exact disjoint branches are
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:first}
\theta_k(x,n)=
\begin{cases}
T_n,&x\in\{0,3,4\},\\
T_n+2d-11,&x=2,\\
T_n+x-4,&5\le x\le d-2,\\
T_n-(d-6),&x=d.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
The same formula holds on row zero except that $\theta_k(k,0)=0$. For the right-marker branch, its creation time is $T_{n-1}+d-6$, and $T_n-T_{n-1}=2d-12$, giving the displayed value. The interior branch is the leading east-moving member's first visit. The site $2$ first appears in the last pre-$L$ return state, whose relative time is $d-5+d-6=2d-11$. These branches partition every visited row even at the smallest gap. They establish piecewise-quadratic first-arrival data directly, without using a certificate compiler.

\section{Exact centered counts and stationary inverse}\label{smc:bp:sec:Gcount}
For integer $N\ge0$, define
\[
C_{G,k}(N)=\#(V_k\cap[-N,N]^2),\qquad
R_{G,k}(M)=\min\{N\in\NN:C_{G,k}(N)\ge M\},\quad M\in\NN.
\]
\begin{theorem}[Stationary count and complete inverse]\label{smc:bp:thm:Gcount}
One has
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:Gcount}
C_{G,k}(N)=
\begin{cases}
1,&N=0,\\
N(N+1),&1\le N\le k-2,\\
\dfrac{N^2+(2k-1)N-k^2+3k-4}{2},&N\ge k-1.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
The exact generalized inverse is
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:Ginverse}
R_{G,k}(M)=
\begin{cases}
0,&0\le M\le1,\\
\left\lceil\dfrac{\sqrt{1+4M}-1}{2}\right\rceil,
&2\le M\le(k-2)(k-1),\\[5pt]
\left\lceil\dfrac{\sqrt{8M+8k^2-16k+17}-(2k-1)}{2}\right\rceil,
&M>(k-2)(k-1).
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Thus $C_{G,k}(N)=\frac12N^2+(k-\frac12)N+O_k(1)$ and
$R_{G,k}(M)=\sqrt{2M}-k+\frac12+O_k(1)$. \wtag\ [The terms $-k+\frac12$ are absorbed by $O_k(1)$; more precisely $R_{G,k}(M)=\sqrt{2M}-k+\frac12+\vartheta_M+o(1)$ with $0\le\vartheta_M<1$, the ceiling's rounding.]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
All visited coordinates are nonnegative. For $1\le N\le k-2$, every included row contributes exactly $N$ sites: $0$ and $2,\ldots,N$. At $N=0$ only the origin contributes.

For $N\ge k$, the full wedge contributes
\[
(N+1)^2-\frac{(N-k)(N-k+1)}2.
\]
Subtract the $N+1$ holes on $x=1$ and the $N-k+2$ holes on $x=k+n-1$. Simplification gives the final branch. At $N=k-1$, the complete square loses $N+1$ sites on $x=1$ and one upper-edge hole; $N(N+1)-1$ agrees with that branch. Omitting either family of holes would produce the wrong polynomial.

The count is strictly increasing, and solving the corresponding quadratic inequality gives the two ceiling formulas. The threshold in~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:Ginverse} places the last inverse in the last count branch. The asymptotics follow by expanding its positive root; integer rounding contributes a bounded error.
\end{proof}

\section{Vertical drift and four disjoint traces}\label{smc:bp:sec:drift}
For each time $t$, let $n(t)$ be the unique integer satisfying $T_{n(t)}\le t<T_{n(t)+1}$. Call the left marker and two matched moving particles the three bulk trajectories. Their $F$ height is
\[
h(t)=t+n(t).
\]
The right marker has $G$ row $r(t)\in\{n(t),n(t)+1\}$, so its $F$ position is $(k+r(t),v(t))$, where $v(t)=t+r(t)$. The functions $n(t)$ and $r(t)$ are nondecreasing, so $h(t)$ and $v(t)$ are strictly increasing.

\begin{lemma}[Disjointness of all four traces]\label{smc:bp:lem:traces}
Each particle's $F$ trace has no self-intersection, and the four traces are pairwise disjoint. Every time contributes four new sites to the all-time visited set.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The strictly increasing heights exclude self-repetition. All three bulk sites at a given time have the same height and distinct horizontal coordinates. Their heights agree across two times only when the times agree, so the three bulk traces are pairwise disjoint.

Suppose a marker site at time $t$ equals a bulk site at time $u$. Write $r=r(t)$ and $n=n(u)$. Every bulk horizontal coordinate is at most $k+n-2$, while the marker coordinate is $k+r$. Equality forces $n\ge r+2$. Height equality gives $u=t+r-n\le t-2$. Monotonicity then implies $n(u)\le n(t)\le r(t)=r$, contradicting $n\ge r+2$. Thus no marker site lies on a bulk trace.
\end{proof}

The three bulk trajectories have one site at every nonnegative height except the skipped levels
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:a}
a_n=T_n+n-1=n^2+(2k-10)n-1,\qquad n\ge1.
\end{equation}
Indeed $n(t)$ rises by one at $T_n$, so $h$ jumps by two there. The right marker rises to row $n+1$ at
\[
\rho_n=T_n+k+n-5.
\]
It therefore skips precisely
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:b}
b_n=\rho_n+n=n^2+(2k-9)n+k-5,\qquad n\ge0.
\end{equation}
Both sequences are strictly increasing. They interlace: $b_0<a_1$, and for $n\ge1$,
\[
a_n<b_n<a_{n+1},\qquad
b_n-a_n=n+k-4,\qquad a_{n+1}-b_n=n+k-5.
\]
No height is skipped by both the bulk and marker families. Notice that ``four new sites per time'' alone does not count sites in a spatial box: the four sites may enter that box at different heights and horizontal positions.

\section{The exact drift count and its inverse}\label{smc:bp:sec:Fcount}
Write $V_{F,k}=\bigcup_{t\ge0}F^t(S_0)$ and define $C_{F,k}(N)$ and $R_{F,k}(M)$ by the same centered-box and generalized-inverse conventions.

\begin{lemma}[The horizontal cutoff]\label{smc:bp:lem:cutoff}
Every $F$-visited site $(x,y)$ satisfies $0\le x\le\max(k+1,y)$ and $y\ge0$. Consequently, for $N\ge k+1$, horizontal truncation removes no visited site of height at most $N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For bulk sites in rounds $n=0,1$, $x\le k+n-2\le k-1$. For $n\ge2$, $T_n\ge T_2=4k-18\ge k-2$, and hence $x\le k+n-2\le T_n+n\le y$.

For the right marker, rows $r\le1$ give $x=k+r\le k+1$. When $r\ge2$, its first time in that row is $\rho_{r-1}=T_{r-1}+k+r-6$, so
\[
y-x\ge T_{r-1}+r-6\ge T_1-4=2k-14\ge0.
\]
All coordinates are nonnegative by the phase formulas and the drift. The first lifted marker has $x=k+1$, so the claimed threshold cannot simply be replaced by $N\ge k$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Floor-root count and inverse]\label{smc:bp:thm:Fcount}
For every integer $N\ge k+1$, let $\alpha=2k-10$ and $\beta=2k-9$. Then
\begin{align}
A_k(N)&=\#\{n\ge1:a_n\le N\}
=\left\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{\alpha^2+4(N+1)}-\alpha}{2}\right\rfloor,\label{smc:bp:eq:A}\\
B_k(N)&=\#\{n\ge0:b_n\le N\}
=1+\left\lfloor\frac{\sqrt{\beta^2+4(N-k+5)}-\beta}{2}\right\rfloor,\label{smc:bp:eq:B}\\
C_{F,k}(N)&=4(N+1)-3A_k(N)-B_k(N)\label{smc:bp:eq:Fcount}\\
&=4N-4\sqrt N+O_k(1).\nonumber
\end{align}
As $M\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:Finverse}
R_{F,k}(M)=\frac M4+\sqrt{\frac M4}+O_k(1).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{smc:bp:lem:cutoff} reduces the box count to a height count. The three disjoint bulk traces each omit $A_k(N)$ of the $N+1$ heights, and the marker trace omits $B_k(N)$. Lemma~\ref{smc:bp:lem:traces} allows these counts to be added without overlap. Solving the two quadratic inequalities gives~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:A}--\eqref{smc:bp:eq:B}; the stated cutoff ensures that their expressions are nonnegative. Each is $\sqrt N+O_k(1)$.

For the inverse, set $m=M/4$ and $u=m+\sqrt m$. Then $u-\sqrt u=m+O(1)$. Rounding $u$ to an integer changes the count by $O_k(1)$. Beyond the transient, increasing $N$ by one adds four sites at a nonskipped height, one at a bulk skip, or three at a marker skip. The interlacing excludes a simultaneous skip. Thus an $O_k(1)$ count discrepancy can be corrected by $O_k(1)$ further radius, proving~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:Finverse}.
\end{proof}

For completeness, the finite transient also has an exact formula requiring no limiting convention:
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:transient}
C_{F,k}(N)=\sum_{t=0}^N\#\{(x,y)\in G^t(S_0):x\le N,\ t+y\le N\}\qquad(N\ge0).
\end{equation}
All coordinates are nonnegative and the traces are disjoint; times $t>N$ have height greater than $N$. Thus~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:transient} follows directly and can use the complete phase formulas. Together with~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:Fcount}, it gives an exact algorithm for every inverse value: check the finite transient, then find the least integer satisfying the monotone floor inequality. The implementation evaluates roots with integer square roots, including exact-square boundaries, rather than floating-point approximations.

\section{Exact Boolean degree and all essential inputs}\label{smc:bp:sec:degree}
For input shape $A$ at anchor $a$, define the exact-component indicator
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:indicator}
M_{A,a}(c)=\prod_{p\in A+a}c_p\prod_{q\in H(A+a)\setminus(A+a)}(1-c_q).
\end{equation}
On binary inputs it equals one precisely for an isolated recognized component. The local rule is the following real multilinear polynomial on binary inputs:
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:poly}
g_z(c)=c_z+
\sum_{(A,B)}\ \sum_{b\in B\setminus A}M_{A,z-b}(c)
-\sum_{(A,B)}\ \sum_{a\in A\setminus B}M_{A,z-a}(c).
\end{equation}
Its validity on the whole Boolean cube follows from exact recognition and disjoint outputs, not merely from checking the selected orbit. There are respectively $2,4,4,6$ signed translated indicators of types $E,W,R,L$. Each halo is a rectangle of height five and width $6,7,8,9$, respectively. The indicator degrees are consequently $30,35,40,45$.

Summing~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:poly} over sites gives a second finite-input conservation proof: each translated-indicator sum is unchanged by the offset, and $|B\setminus A|=|A\setminus B|$. Only finitely many indicators can be nonzero on a finite support, so these sum rearrangements are legitimate.

\begin{theorem}[Exact degree and exact dependency set]\label{smc:bp:thm:degree}
The unique real multilinear polynomial for the local Boolean rule has degree exactly 45. Every cell of $D_G$ is essential and no other cell is; $F$ has exactly the translated dependency set $D_F$. Both specified rules therefore have exact centered Chebyshev radius six.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The polynomial~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:poly} is multilinear because each indicator uses every input bit at most once. The six $L$ indicators have distinct rectangular halos of size 45. Each has three occupied factors and 42 complementary factors, so its degree-45 top coefficient is $(-1)^{42}=1$, multiplied by the birth or removal sign. Their top monomials are distinct and cannot cancel one another; every other indicator has degree at most 40. This proves exact degree 45 by uniqueness of multilinear interpolation on the Boolean cube.

For the three births, the $L$ halos have horizontal intervals $[-2,6]$, $[-5,3]$, $[-6,2]$ and vertical interval $[-3,1]$. For the three removals, the horizontal intervals are $[-2,6]$, $[-4,4]$, $[-6,2]$ and the vertical interval is $[-2,2]$. Their union is exactly $D_G$. Every one of its 78 variables therefore occurs in a nonzero degree-45 monomial.

If a Boolean function were independent of one such variable, the difference between the polynomial's one and zero restrictions would vanish on the Boolean cube. Uniqueness of multilinear interpolation would force this derivative polynomial to vanish identically, contradicting the nonzero monomial containing that variable. Thus all 78 variables are essential. Section~\ref{smc:bp:sec:locality} excludes any other dependency. Translating the evaluation point gives $D_F$ and its essentiality. Both rectangles contain a horizontal coordinate of absolute value six, which proves the exact centered radius.
\end{proof}

The release also supplies an explicit finite witness for each cell: two configurations differing at that cell only, with different central outputs. The file \file{essential-input-witnesses.json} in \file{scientific/audit/} records all 78 pairs. They are checked with both the direct component simulator and the independently encoded cylinder evaluator. Translating both inputs down one row gives the corresponding $F$ witness. This is a concrete flip-witness confirmation independent of relying on the top-monomial argument. Exact radius here is a property of these two rules, not a least-radius claim over all possible binary constructions.

\section{A literal one step quartic with unique auxiliaries}\label{smc:bp:sec:quartic}
Enumerate the 78 cells of $D_G$ in row-major order, increasing $y$ and then $x$, and add one external output variable $y_{\rm out}$. There are 79 external variables. For each of the sixteen indicators, use its own unshared multiplication chain, listing occupied factors first and complementary factors afterward. Within each group use row-major order. Start the chain with its first external occupied factor. At every subsequent factor introduce one new auxiliary $z$ and one residual
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:gates}
z-uv\quad\hbox{or}\quad z-u(1-v),
\end{equation}
where $u$ is the preceding chain value and $v$ is the next external input. Indicator order and all variable indices are recorded canonically in the certificate.

There are
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:aux}
2\cdot29+4\cdot34+4\cdot39+6\cdot44=614
\end{equation}
auxiliaries and the same number of gate residuals. Add the 78 bit residuals $c_i(c_i-1)$ and the output residual
\begin{equation}\label{smc:bp:eq:outres}
y_{\rm out}-c_{(0,0)}-\sum_{\rm births}z_{\rm last}+\sum_{\rm removals}z_{\rm last}.
\end{equation}
Let $P$ be the sum of squares of these $614+78+1=693$ residuals. The circuit gives an ordinary integer polynomial, not a compressed truth predicate or a symbolic placeholder for an unspecified computation.
\wtag\ [Arithmetizing a Boolean circuit by bit equations and product gates is the standard device of Part~IV's gate residuals (Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:presburger-quartic}); the counts 614, 693 and 3403 are source~21's own.]

\begin{theorem}[The local zero set and canonical witnesses]\label{smc:bp:thm:quartic}
The total degree of $P$ is exactly four. For any fixed external input-output tuple, its natural auxiliary fiber has exactly one element if that tuple is a valid binary local transition, and has no element otherwise. The same statement holds when the auxiliaries range over all real numbers.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Every residual has degree at most two, so $P$ has degree at most four. The squares of bit residuals supply positive fourth-power monomials of the external input variables, which cannot be canceled by the other squared residuals here; hence the degree is exactly four.

Since $P$ is a sum of real squares, $P=0$ is equivalent to simultaneous vanishing of every residual. The bit residuals force each external input into $\{0,1\}$. Each acyclic chain equation then forces its next auxiliary uniquely, starting from an external input. All forced values are themselves zero or one. Finally~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:outres} forces the output to equal~\eqref{smc:bp:eq:poly}, hence the actual binary local output. Conversely, for a valid transition these forced chain values make every residual zero.

Therefore the unique real auxiliary tuple is natural, and invalid external tuples have no real or natural auxiliary solution. This argument proves uniqueness by the specific deterministic chain structure. It is not an inference from finite perturbation tests or from decidability.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The exact algebra ledger}
There are $2\cdot1+4\cdot1+4\cdot2+6\cdot2=26$ plain-product gates, each with two residual monomials, and $2\cdot28+4\cdot33+4\cdot37+6\cdot42=588$ complementary-factor gates, each with three. The bit residuals each have two monomials and the output residual has 18. Thus the uncollected ledgers are
\begin{align*}
\text{residual-monomial occurrences}&=26\cdot2+588\cdot3+78\cdot2+18=1990,\\
\text{ordered square-expansion occurrences}&=26\cdot4+588\cdot9+78\cdot4+18^2=6032.
\end{align*}
Collecting equal monomials in the literal exported polynomial gives Table~\ref{smc:bp:tab:ledger}. The last three values were independently reconstructed and compared exactly, not assumed from the circuit description.

\begin{table}[htbp]\centering
\begin{tabular}{lr}\toprule
Resource & Exact value\\\midrule
External input bits & 78\\
External variables including output & 79\\
Canonical natural auxiliaries & 614\\
Total variables & 693\\
Squared residuals & 693\\
Residual-monomial occurrences & 1990\\
Ordered square-expansion occurrences & 6032\\
Distinct collected monomials & 3403\\
Largest absolute collected coefficient & 2\\
Total degree & 4\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Literal resource ledger for the deliberately unshared one-step circuit. No auxiliary-minimality claim is made.}\label{smc:bp:tab:ledger}
\end{table}

The machine-readable \file{scientific/local-quartic-certificate.json} contains all 3403 collected terms, the full variable order, all 614 gates, all 693 residuals, and all sixteen indicators. A monomial is a list of variable indices with repetitions representing powers; coefficients are exact integers. The independent artifact reader re-expands squared residuals using unordered pairs and compares the resulting sparse polynomial exactly. A second reconstruction begins from the geometric primitive replacements and independently rebuilds indicators, gates, residuals, and every coefficient. Thus the expanded artifact is checked against both its circuit and its intended geometry.

\section{Verification and portable reproduction}\label{smc:bp:sec:verification}
The release separates proofs from executable evidence. The reviewed source proofs \file{PROOF.md} and \file{LOCAL_ALGEBRA.md} are preserved under \file{scientific/}. This report supplies a connected mathematical presentation. Independent reviews and explicit flip witnesses accompany the source files. No finite regression is offered as an enumeration of all full-shift configurations.

\subsection{Independent routes and finite coverage}
The direct simulator \file{component_rule.py} builds occupied components and applies the four replacements. The pointwise evaluator \file{local_rule.py} imports neither that simulator nor its rule dictionary; it encodes the sixteen occupied-and-empty cylinders independently and exports exact integer masks. Both $G$ and $F$ are evaluated.

The primary suite checks all 4096 supports in a $4\times3$ rectangle, all 8192 supports in a 13-site line, arbitrary exterior contexts, one-site halo interference, interacting active and malformed templates, births over a full-radius candidate region, large translations, exact orbit phases, first arrivals, both box counts, disjoint traces, and integer-root boundary cases. Its deterministic receipt reports 76680 direct orbit phase steps, 46980 first-arrival checks, and 53601 drift count checks. These are finite checks of the displayed formulas, not substitutes for the all-input proofs.

The quartic artifact reader checks 350 binary local fixtures and their incorrect binary outputs. At the vacuum witness it tests all 614 upward unit perturbations, one per auxiliary. It also compares the sparse identity at 60 nonbinary integer points and obtains all 78 essentiality witnesses. These tests do not exhaust all auxiliary perturbations on all inputs; uniqueness is proved in Theorem~\ref{smc:bp:thm:quartic}. Independent component, drift, cross-implementation, and geometry-to-quartic reconstruction checks provide separate routes. Every scientific test used by the release replay uses explicit exception checks, so optimization does not disable its checks. Normal and \file{-O} runs must agree with the exact expected receipts, including JSON value types.

\subsection{Integrity before execution and isolated outputs}
The portable verifier first compares the exact required payload file set and independently pinned SHA-256 values before executing any packaged scientific code. It then runs in fresh external copies. Adjacent-output scripts are executed only in those copies. Expected receipts are compared recursively with exact types, so a Boolean is not accepted in place of an integer. The verifier checks that release bytes, file modes, and modification times are unchanged after the run. The release checksum list and archive checksum provide an additional external integrity layer; executing the verifier still presumes trust in that bootstrap file.

The README supplies exact commands and requirements. Scientific replay needs Python 3.10 or later and only the standard library. PDF rebuild needs \file{pdflatex} and the listed TeX packages; figure regeneration additionally needs Matplotlib. Supplied vector PDF figures allow a report rebuild without Matplotlib. Build products and replay receipts belong outside the release. Bounded tamper regressions test changed code, missing required files, altered certificate content, and strict typed comparisons; they do not run the scientific suite on tampered payloads.

The portable package contains no Python bytecode caches, private absolute paths, third-party source caches, or files from earlier releases. Those earlier releases are cited, not modified. The exact source and receipt lineage is recorded in \file{verification/source-lineage.json}.

\wtag\ \textbf{The release in this report.} These are the delivered names. \path{build.sh}, \path{reproduce.sh}, \path{replay.py}, \path{tamper_regression.py} and \path{figures/make_figures.py} are shipped as \path{code/21-planar-shuttle-*}, the programs of \path{scientific/code/} as \path{code/21-planar-shuttle-code-*} and those of \path{scientific/audit/} as \path{code/21-planar-shuttle-audit-*}; the receipts, review bindings and certificates of \path{scientific/audit/} as \path{data/21-planar-shuttle-audit-*}, the two certificates of \path{scientific/} (among them \path{local-quartic-certificate.json}), the figure data and the \path{verification/} records as \path{data/21-planar-shuttle-*}; the two figures as \path{figures/21-planar-shuttle-rule.pdf} and \path{figures/21-planar-shuttle-orbits.pdf}; and \path{scientific/PROOF.md}, \path{LOCAL_ALGEBRA.md} and the four audit notes as \path{21-planar-shuttle-*.md} at the report root. Not shipped: the manuscript (printed here), its PDF, README and checksum ledger. Several delivered receipts are byte copies of one another (normal, optimized and expected copies); they are shipped as delivered, because the replay compares them by name. At placement on Windows every replay step passed in normal and optimized mode after two workarounds for harness defects, which the README states: \path{replay.py} starts its children with \texttt{-I}, which ignores \texttt{PYTHONUTF8}, so two audits fail to print non-ASCII text on a cp1252 console unless run with \texttt{-X utf8}; and several writers emit CRLF line ends on Windows, so the byte comparison of the rebuilt local quartic certificate fails although its content equals the delivered file.

\wtag\ \textbf{The programme's review.} The Hilbert's-tenth programme reviewed this archive in \path{review_binary_planar_four_particle31.md}, with its checker and receipt, in \path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/research-wip/native-stream-queue/} (commit \texttt{16f50dcc6}, 3 October 2026): ``PASS within the stated scope; no error found''. It read the proof notes in full, authenticated the archive and seven members, and, from the four printed replacement rules alone, rebuilt the sixteen indicators, the 32 recognition cylinders of $G$ and $F$, the 614 product-chain gates and the 693 residuals; their expansion matches all 3403 saved terms, of degree four. It confirms the six noncancelling degree-45 leaders, the 78 essential cells and exact radius six for these rules, the phase intervals including $k=7$, both missing lines, the cutoff $N\ge k+1$, and both drift asymptotics, and adds 483 phase states, 183 first arrivals and 261 box counts for $k=7,8,11$. It executed none of the archive's Python, and it notes that the 614 auxiliaries are not a complete evaluation cost of the quartic and that the rule gives no universal simulation; the repository's universal operation bounds are unchanged.

\section{Consequences limits and next questions}\label{smc:bp:sec:limits}
This example realizes two distinct counting regimes with an ordinary binary mass-four orbit: a quadratic stationary-frame wedge count with sharp coefficient $1/2$, and a linear drifted count with a square-root deficit of coefficient four. The deficit records skipped heights of four disjoint traces, rather than weighted-site multiplicities. The two removed wedge lines and the finite horizontal cutoff are essential to the exact formulas.

The construction proves existence for the specified initial family, not a classification of arbitrary malformed orbits. Malformed inputs are covered by the conservation and locality proof; they are not claimed to enter a shuttle. Neither $G$ nor $F$ is asserted reversible or universal. No smallest mass, smallest radius among constructions, minimal number of essential cells, optimal Boolean degree, or optimal auxiliary count is asserted.

The local quartic relates 78 input bits to one next-step output. Its unique real auxiliary tuple follows from acyclic multiplication chains and bit equations. It does not imply an unbounded-time finite-fold representation for arbitrary recursively enumerable sets. Report 30 separately proves that, for a fixed promised conservative CA and fixed mass-at-most-four input, untimed reachability of a canonical \emph{complete configuration} has an effective ordinary quartic with one natural witness tuple, optionally including first occurrence time~\textup{[source~20, Part~\ref{smc:zd:part}]}. That theorem uses the full classified timed charts and whole-orbit injectivity in the expanding case. Its hypotheses include this example, but its general certificate is not the 614-auxiliary local polynomial here. Site or partial-pattern recurrence cannot be substituted for complete-target injectivity, and no real-witness conclusion is inherited from that theorem.

Natural next questions remain tightly connected to this construction:
\begin{enumerate}
\item\label{smc:bp:q:radius} Can a binary planar four-particle wedge be realized with a smaller centered radius or fewer essential cells while retaining a transparent all-input conservation proof? (\wtag\ Open. Compare source~17's Question~\ref{smc:fm:q:radius} for the one-dimensional rule, which stays open too.)
\item\label{smc:bp:q:shared} Can shared products reduce the 614 local auxiliaries without introducing noncanonical inactive values, and what exact collected polynomial results?
\item\label{smc:bp:q:bounces} Which changes to the two transverse bounces preserve four-trace disjointness after drift, and which change the coefficient of the square-root deficit?
\end{enumerate}
These are questions, not claims settled by the present finite checks or by the existing general classification.


\appendix
\section{Part~I: a minimal worked use of the compiler}
\label{smc:cg:app:example}

The following code can be run from the archive root. (\wtag\ ``The archive root'' is the delivered layout, with \path{code/signal_certificates.py}; in this report the module is shipped as \path{code/07-collision-geometry-signal_certificates.py}, so the snippet runs in a copy that restores the delivered name (README of this report).) It builds the
first-collision tournament and constructs a complete certificate without
using an external algebra system.
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python]
import sys
sys.path.insert(0, "code")
from signal_certificates import Machine, compile_skeleton

machine = Machine(
    {"A": 2, "B": 0, "C": -1, "H": 0, "R": 0},
    {
        frozenset(("A", "B")): ("H",),
        frozenset(("B", "C")): ("R",),
        frozenset(("A", "B", "C")): ("R",),
    },
)
skeleton = (((0, 1),),)
cert = compile_skeleton(machine, ("A", "B", "C"), skeleton)
gaps = (1, 1)
slacks = cert.witnesses(gaps)
assert cert.evaluate(gaps, slacks) == 0
assert not cert.accepts((3, 1))  # An earlier BC collision.
assert not cert.accepts((2, 1))  # A simultaneous ABC collision.
print(cert.to_dict())
\end{lstlisting}
The order of rows in the exported matrices is lexicographic and need not
match the order used in the displayed mathematical formula. Row order
changes only the order of slack coordinates, not the represented relation
or the uniqueness statement.

\section{Part~II: replay and build guide}\label{smc:sd:app:replay}
The accompanying archive contains this article in editable LaTeX, the compiled PDF, exact Python scripts, expected receipts, example coefficient dictionaries, and a manifest with file hashes. It contains no third-party source PDFs. The scripts require Python 3.10 or later and only the standard library. Assertions must remain enabled; do not run Python with \texttt{-O}.

From the extracted package root run:
\begin{verbatim}
python3 scripts/run_replay.py
\end{verbatim}
The runner copies the executable scripts into a temporary directory, executes the suites there, compares fresh JSON receipts to the stored expectations, and writes a summary under \texttt{replay-output}. The stored expected receipts and packaged examples are not overwritten. Use \texttt{--output DIR} to select a different result directory. The worked-example receipt also contains its concrete witness and explicit rejection reason for the false final-cut schema.


\wtag\ These are the delivered names. In this report the five scripts are shipped as \path{code/11-signal-certificates-*.py}, \path{build.sh} as \path{code/11-signal-certificates-build.sh}, the two example files and the manifest as \path{data/11-signal-certificates-*.json}, and the four expected receipts as \path{data/11-signal-certificates-expected-*_receipt.json}. The runner looks for \path{scripts/}, \path{examples/} and \path{expected_receipts/} in the parent of its own directory and runs the scripts by their delivered names, so it runs only in a copy that restores the delivered layout (see the README); it writes nothing into the shipped files. Of the archive contents listed above, the editable source is printed as this Part rather than shipped separately, and the compiled PDF and the checksum file \path{CHECKSUMS.sha256} are not shipped (the checksum file was verified, 17 of 17 files, at placement); the manifest is shipped, and its payload list still names the delivered paths and the delivered PDF and article source.

The main executable interfaces are:
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{signal\_geometry.py}: exact simulator, full-layer compiler, polynomial evaluation and rational Gaussian rank calculation;
\item \texttt{signal\_sparse.py}: event-coordinate and adjacency-lifetime compiler;
\item \texttt{verify\_additional.py}: separately seeded edge cases and sharp height checks;
\item \texttt{verify\_examples.py}: the article's explicit examples and convention checks.
\end{itemize}
The low-level compilers use assertions to reject malformed schema partitions or structurally impossible collision directions. They are reference mathematical implementations, not hardened parsers for untrusted inputs. JSON residual dictionaries use \texttt{@} for the constant term, \texttt{x0}, \texttt{x1}, and so on for input positions, and the listed variable names for natural witnesses. A certificate evaluates to the sum of the squares of those rows. No expanded polynomial is required to replay it.

To rebuild the article run:
\begin{verbatim}
sh build.sh
\end{verbatim}
A working TeX Live installation with \texttt{pdflatex}, Latin Modern fonts, AMS packages, \texttt{geometry}, \texttt{microtype}, \texttt{booktabs}, \texttt{enumitem} and \texttt{hyperref} suffices. The build script uses fixed document metadata and a fixed source-date epoch; exact PDF byte equality across different TeX distributions is not promised. The mathematical replay receipts are exact and version-independent within supported Python semantics.

\section{Part~II: claim and source guide}\label{smc:sd:app:sources}
The source-supported context is limited to the following claims. All certificate theorems and proofs in this article are supplied here rather than attributed to the literature.
\begin{itemize}
\item Durand-Lose 2009: finite configurations in Definition~1; integer inputs in Section~2; speeds in Figure~4; cyclic-tag simulation and halting in Section~5 and Theorem~5. Common-denominator rescaling is our inference, proved in \eqref{smc:sd:eq:normalize}.
\item Durand-Lose 2007: linear-register event simulation in Section~4 of the author version, especially its ninth PDF page; rational models in Section~5. No Diophantine theorem is attributed to it.
\item Durand-Lose 2006: energy and live-population bounds in Definition~3 and the following paragraph; accumulation qualifications in Sections~2.2 and~6.
\item Ginsburg and Spanier 1966: equivalence of Presburger-definable and semilinear subsets of $\NN^n$, Theorem~1.3, pp.~288--289.
\end{itemize}
Primary sources were checked on 2 October 2026. The bibliography links to the papers or their official bibliographic records; copyright-bearing PDFs are not redistributed in this package.
