Source~07's pin is the full commit \texttt{f1edb38f93aa0a362a34b531a600909f6e74608a}, named in its Section~\ref{smc:cg:sec:implementation} and in its delivered \path{PROVENANCE.md} (shipped as \path{07-collision-geometry-PROVENANCE.md}); it is an ancestor of the placement commit, and the audit blob it cites is unchanged at the write. Source~11 names no repository snapshot and cites no repository file; its only repository-related statement, that the 75- and 87-operation constructions are not improved, is true at the write.

\paragraph{Where the merge had to choose.}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Base and order.} Source~07 is the base (Part~I): it allows signed rational speeds without a normalization interface, proves more about the chamber, and is the longer manuscript. Source~11 follows as Part~II. Every result, proof, example, remark, research question, appendix and limitation of both sources is printed, in the sources' order and with their numbering inside each Part.
\item \emph{The common theorem.} Its two statements have different inputs and witnesses, so both are printed, each with its proof. The lemmas of Part~II that coincide with lemmas of Part~I (Lemmas~\ref{smc:sd:lem:denom}, \ref{smc:sd:lem:lifetimes} and~\ref{smc:sd:lem:interior}, Proposition~\ref{smc:sd:prop:semilinear}) open with a \wtag\ note naming their Part~I counterpart, and source~11's proofs are kept as second routes. Section~\ref{smc:sec:routes} compares the two routes, including the two shuttles and the two missed-simultaneity examples, which are kept in both Parts because they are different machines.
\item \emph{A result already in the collection.} Part~I's Theorem~\ref{smc:cg:thm:rigidity} and Corollary~\ref{smc:cg:cor:barrier} are a special case of Part~XI of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates}. They are printed with source~07's statements and proofs, as a second route, and credited in Remark~\ref{smc:cg:rem:credit}; credit notes were added to source~07's abstract and conclusion. Remark~\ref{smc:cg:rem:singlefold} relates the first-hit quartic to the same Part.
\item \emph{Research questions.} Both agendas are printed in their Parts; Table~\ref{smc:tab:questions} pairs the coinciding ones, and one \wtag\ note re-scopes the part of Part~II's Question~\ref{smc:sd:q:ledger} that both sources already prove.
\item \emph{Front matter and bibliography.} The two title pages are replaced by one; each source's title, author line, abstract and keywords open its Part. Section~\ref{smc:sec:front} is new. The two bibliographies are merged: source~11's keys \texttt{DL09} and \texttt{GS66} are cited as source~07's \texttt{durandlose} and \texttt{ginsburgspanier}, whose entries now carry source~11's editors and links; entries~\cite{cdc,lbh,review808,reviewsignal,blockca} were added for the cross-references.
\item \emph{Typesetting.} Equations are numbered by section in both Parts (source~07 numbered them consecutively). Source~11's macro \verb|\N| is written \verb|\NN| because source~07's \verb|\N| prints $\N$; both print the natural numbers $\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$ as their sources did. No symbol was renamed in the printed text and no normalization changed.
\item \emph{Added text.} Every sentence written in the merge is marked \wtag: Section~\ref{smc:sec:front}; the openings of the two Parts; notes in Part~I in Sections~\ref{smc:cg:sec:intro}, \ref{smc:cg:sec:sparse}, \ref{smc:cg:sec:quadratic}, \ref{smc:cg:sec:depth}, \ref{smc:cg:sec:counting} (and Remark~\ref{smc:cg:rem:singlefold}), \ref{smc:cg:sec:tournament}, \ref{smc:cg:sec:clock}, \ref{smc:cg:sec:barrier} (Remark~\ref{smc:cg:rem:credit}), \ref{smc:cg:sec:implementation} (shipped file names, the pin), \ref{smc:cg:sec:future} and \ref{smc:cg:sec:conclusion}, and in its abstract; notes in Part~II in Sections~\ref{smc:sd:sec:purpose}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:model}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:normalization}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:sparse}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:proof}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:examples}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:height}, \ref{smc:sd:sec:fixed} and \ref{smc:sd:sec:verification}; notes in the four source appendices; this appendix; and the bibliography entries marked \wtag. Labels were added to the first lemma of Part~I's Section~\ref{smc:cg:sec:sparse}, to one example subsection, the research directions, the conclusion and the second appendix of Part~I, and to several sections, one corollary, the questions, the conclusion and the second appendix of Part~II.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Files.} The report directory holds this text (\path{article.tex}, \texttt{article.pdf}), a README, source~07's \path{PROVENANCE.md} and source~11's \path{REPRODUCIBILITY.md} (as \path{07-collision-geometry-PROVENANCE.md} and \path{11-signal-certificates-REPRODUCIBILITY.md}), and the two sources' programs in \path{code/} and data in \path{data/}, under the prefixes \path{07-collision-geometry-} and \path{11-signal-certificates-}. The README maps each delivered name to its shipped name and gives rerun instructions that do not overwrite the shipped files. Source~11's manuscript, both PDFs, source~11's delivered README and both checksum ledgers (source~07's \path{SHA256SUMS}, 14 of 14 files, and source~11's \path{CHECKSUMS.sha256}, 17 of 17, both verified at placement) are not shipped; they survive in the archives of commit \texttt{808b53ed8}. Source~07's delivered README was the placed \path{README.md} and has been replaced by the report README.

\paragraph{Where the batch-79 addition had to choose.}

Every result, proof, example, remark, question, appendix and limitation of both is printed, in the sources' order.
\item \emph{The two editions of source~12.} The corrected edition is printed; its article is identical to the earlier edition's. The correction concerns only the generic evaluator program and is described in Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:replay}.
\item \emph{Author lines.} Source~12's title page describes it as a ``Research note prepared with AI assistance for private review''; as the report is public, Part~III's opening describes it neutrally as an AI-assisted research note, and the delivered wording is recorded here. Source~13's author line, ``Research report prepared with AI assistance for Vladimir'', is quoted in Part~IV's opening.
\item \emph{Re-derived results.} Part~III's event lemma and uniform lift, Part~IV's comparison and sorting gadgets, its post-elimination quartic and the finite-search step of its cutoff proposition re-derive results of Parts~I--II or of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates}. They are printed as delivered, with \wtag\ notes naming the earlier result, as second routes; no novelty is claimed for them. The selector/copy construction of Part~III is credited to Balas and to Jeroslow and Lowe.

\item \emph{Labels.} Source~12's 49 labels are \texttt{smc:cs:} plus their delivered names, and source~13's 53 are \texttt{smc:sl:} plus theirs; none was dropped or renamed apart from the prefix. The labels added in batch~79 (the two Parts and the five subsections of Section~\ref{smc:sec:front}) also use \texttt{smc:cs:}, although the subsections cover both Parts.

\section{Part~III: a small complete quadratic packet}\label{smc:cs:app:example}
Consider the one-dimensional partial-branch presentation of the total map $F(x)=2x$ on $\NN$: branch 0 has guard $x=0$ and update 0; branch 1 has guard $x>0$ and update $2x$. The packet has selectors $b_0,b_1$, copies $z_0,z_1$, and one slack $s$:
\begin{align*}
 P(x,y;b_0,b_1,z_0,z_1,s)
 ={}&(b_0+b_1-1)^2+(x-z_0-z_1)^2+(y-2z_1)^2\\
   &+z_0^2+(z_1-b_1-s)^2+b_1z_0+b_0z_1.
\end{align*}
Here $B=2$, $d=1$, $E=1$, $T=1$, $W=0$, so the ledger gives five variables, five squared residuals, and two products. For $x=0,y=0$ the unique witness is $(1,0,0,0,0)$; for $x>0,y=2x$ it is $(0,1,0,x,x-1)$. Every other input/output pair has no natural witness. The core replay exhaustively checks 100,000 bounded assignments, including every auxiliary tuple in $\{0,\ldots,4\}^5$ for $0\le x<4$, $0\le y<8$.

The generic event-branch compiler also fully enumerates a three-signal transparent test machine with speeds $2,0,-2$. Its 19 branches have $E=20$, $T=84$, $W=0$, yielding 160 auxiliary variables, 111 squared affine residuals, and 19 complementarity products. Both pivot and uniform scalings pass 144 valid-germ tests, including 88 legal steps and two simultaneous steps, with every canonical residual exactly zero.

This example illustrates the exact polynomial mechanics without suggesting that a universal branch family has only two branches. For the signal frontend, the same constructor takes the actual finite label-mode and first-collision branch descriptions.

\section{Part~III: the complete finite mode closure and numeric packet}\label{smc:cs:app:closure}
\subsection{A finite family covering every encoded run}
The naive family of all $114^{18}$ ordered label words is unnecessary. Starting from the literal initial mode, consider each nonempty subset $J$ of eligible adjacent closing pairs. A pair is eligible only when its exact incoming labels have a defined rule and their speeds are in incoming order. Exclude subsets with consecutive edges, since those would constitute an undefined collision of at least three signals. Replace every selected pair atomically by its speed-sorted output pair and add the resulting mode. Repeating to exhaustion gives the least mode-only closure for this syntactic successor operation.

Every legal event of an encoded run uses one of these nonadjacent eligible subsets, by the complete-collision lemma. Induction therefore places every event mode and branch in the closure. Conversely, every retained branch has exactly the original mode, germ, earliest-collision and rule guards, so it introduces no spurious geometric step. Restricting the integer map to this closed mode family preserves every encoded computation.

The closure is an overapproximation of modes numerically reachable from the specified input family: successive graph edges may be realizable at different gap vectors which cannot be spliced into one orbit. No assertion of reachability of every graph node is made. Nevertheless every branch is individually feasible. If $c$ is its speed-difference vector, choose
\[
 h_i=\begin{cases}c_i&i\in J,\\ \max(c_i,0)+1&i\notin J,\end{cases}
 \qquad u=q\sum_i h_i.
\]
All gaps are strictly positive, its next delay is one, exactly $J$ collides, and the output is an admissible germ. The exporter checks this witness for every branch.

The completed enumeration has 49,700 modes and 80,501 branches: 65,557 single-collision branches and 14,944 branches with two simultaneous disjoint collisions. The maximum graph discovery depth is 321. This depth is a graph statistic, not a computation-time bound or a numeric reachability certificate.

Among the modes, 1,728 have no eligible legal pair. Only 36 of those have no closing edge at all and are collision-free; the other 1,692 are blocked by undefined collisions. Of the 36 collision-free modes, six carry an accepting halt label and 30 carry the null label. The fixed final accepting mode in Section~\ref{smc:cs:sec:integer} is the target for encoded runs. Neither all graph sinks nor the union of those six label-bearing modes replaces that target.

\subsection{Exact guard and witness counts}
For each branch the exported guard convention retains one mode equality, $|J|-1$ nonpivot tie equalities, one strict germ guard for each $c_i\ge0$, the strict span guard, the strict pivot-gap guard, and all $17-|J|$ non-$J$ endpoint guards. Some strict guards are redundant; retaining them makes the convention simple and fully reproducible. Nonnegative coordinates are the ambient natural domain, so no weak-gap slacks are introduced.

The resulting exact ledger is
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lr}
\toprule
Quantity & Count\\
\midrule
Branches $B$ & 80,501\\
Equality guards $E$ & 95,445\\
Strict guards $T$ & 2,667,479\\
Weak guards $W$ & 0\\
Auxiliary variables $19B+T$ & 4,196,998\\
All variables including $X,Y$ & 4,197,034\\
Squared affine residuals $37+E+T$ & 2,762,961\\
Complementarity products & 80,501\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Thus the literal one-step polynomial is fully specified, quadratic, nonnegative on natural arguments, and canonical by Theorem~\ref{smc:cs:thm:packet}. For externally fixed $K$ and supplied endpoints, its unrolling has exactly $4{,}197{,}016K-18$ witness variables. This explicit constant is large; the small physical population does not imply a comparably small Diophantine arity.

The untimed affine rows contain 15,323,489 nonzero variable-coefficient entries and one nonzero constant. The maximum absolute affine coefficient has 124 bits. Gap coefficients remain bounded by 24; the larger values arise from finite mode coding. These are sparse affine-circuit statistics, not claims about minimal gate complexity. Adding the optional timestamp coordinate retains the same branch guards and adds one local copy coordinate per branch and two global input/output residuals. Its derived ledger is 4,277,499 auxiliaries and 2,762,963 squared affine residuals per step.

\subsection{What the numeric export contains}
The mode file gives each mode's 18 label indices, speed differences, exact code and graph index. The compact event file records every branch index, source/target index, canonical pivot and bitmask of the complete set $J$. Together with the explicit formulas above, these fixed numbers determine every sparse matrix and guard coefficient. The supplied exporter generates the full numeric branch records, including selector, copy and slack indices. A separate circuit file fixes the global variable order and the polynomial~\eqref{smc:cs:eq:polynomial}. All large codes and generated coefficients are serialized as decimal strings.

The shared selector sum is an expression, not another witness variable. Writing its difference with $b_r$ is exactly $\sum_{h\ne r}b_h$, which is nonnegative on all natural assignments. Keeping this expression factored avoids a quadratic-in-$B$ expansion of the complementarity part. The exporter can regenerate every squared affine residual row or answer individual expanded coefficient queries. The verbose branch and full residual-row files were generated and checked during preparation. They are omitted from the compact archive because they are deterministically regenerable from its complete mode/event data and code.

The supplementary incidence calculation gives 1,059,563,096,023 nonzero terms in the full monomial expansion and a maximum absolute coefficient of 248 bits. Those expansion statistics are derived exactly; no trillion-term monomial file was emitted. The actually generated full affine-residual export was checked independently, including all 15,323,489 nonzero variable-coefficient entries.

This is an explicit coefficient description of one fixed step polynomial and each fixed-horizon family. It does not convert the unknown horizon into a fixed number of natural variables, and it does not assert that the mode graph's finite size bounds the length of a universal run.

\wtag\ In this report the machine file, the ledger and the circuit file are shipped as \path{data/12-conservative-signal-MORITA_18_SIGNAL_MACHINE.json}, \path{data/12-conservative-signal-LEDGER.json} and \path{data/12-conservative-signal-POLYNOMIAL_CIRCUIT.json}, and the exporter as \path{code/12-conservative-signal-compile_packet.py}. The mode file and the compact event file (\path{numeric/MODES.jsonl.gz} and \path{numeric/EVENTS.jsonl.gz} in the delivered layout) are not shipped; the exporter's \texttt{build} command regenerates them with identical decompressed content (README of this report). The programme's review~\cite{reviewcs} enumerated the same 49,700 modes and 80,501 branches independently and derived every entry of the ledger above from the closure.
\section{Part~VII: canonical timed quartic certificates (source~19)}\label{smc:zd:app:quartic}
The preceding proof supplies more than reachability decidability: it produces exact phase data with a rigid algebraic form. We now apply the constant term lifting compiler already established in Report 13 \textup{[source~18, Section~\ref{smc:tq:sec:compiler}]}. Its one dimensional conclusion is not new here. The finite dimensional extension comes from the proved normal form and a disjoint lexicographic sorting refinement. The complete companion proof and its independent conditional review are included in \file{scientific/timed/}. \wtag\ [Shipped as \path{19-mass-four-zd-timed-PROOF.md} and \path{19-mass-four-zd-timed-REVIEW.md}; with this appendix the fixed-input answer to source~17's Question~\ref{smc:fm:q:quartic} holds in every finite dimension.]

\begin{theorem}[Fixed input timed charts and quartics]\label{smc:zd:thm:quartic}
Fix a CA and input satisfying Theorem~\ref{smc:zd:thm:main}. There is an effective finite family of disjoint charts, each with at most two natural parameters and an integer affine domain, whose time and original frame occupied coordinates are rational polynomials of degree at most two, integer valued on their domains. Occupied sites are strictly lexicographically ordered. The disjoint chart domains map bijectively to the complete timed orbit.

For every fixed tuple $\mathbf a=(a_1,\ldots,a_m)$ of nonzero labels, $0\le m\le4$, there is an effectively obtained integer polynomial
\[
 C_{\mathbf a}(t,X_1,\ldots,X_m;w)
\]
of ordinary total degree at most four, with natural witnesses $w$, whose witness fiber is a singleton exactly when $F^t(c)$ has precisely those lexicographically ordered sites and labels, and is empty otherwise. Here $t\in\NN$ and $X_i\in\Z^d$ are external arguments. The witness count depends on the fixed input and rule, not on time or the queried coordinates.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Half open charts own every time exactly once}
\wtag\ [This subsection is source~18's Section~\ref{smc:tq:sec:partition} in the notation of Section~\ref{smc:zd:sec:shuttle} (here $q_\ell$ is source~18's prefix length $b_\ell$, and $C$ is its $B$); new here are the original-frame coordinates in $\Z^d$ and the raw chart count $J$. It is printed because the counts below use it.]
Materialize the finite prefix as one parameter free chart for each $0\le t<T_0$. For a translated periodic or independent tail, extend the prefix through all individual transients and choose one common period $p$. The phase $r$ has the one parameter formula
\[
 t=T_0+r+pn,\qquad 0\le r<p,\quad n\in\NN,
\]
with affine coordinates. Euclidean division determines a unique phase and parameter. The vacuum uses a one clock tail with no occupied coordinates. In the no unit case the same construction already works in the original frame.

For an expanding shuttle, retain the same cycle indexing as in Section~\ref{smc:zd:sec:shuttle}. Let transition $\ell$ have flight length $h_\ell(n)=a_\ell n+b_\ell$ and fixed encounter prefix length $q_\ell\ge0$. The flight length is integral for all natural $n$, so $b_\ell=h_\ell(0)$ and $a_\ell=h_\ell(1)-h_\ell(0)$ are integers. Both $h_\ell(n)>0$ and $a_\ell>0$. The cycle start is
\[
 T(n)=T_0+A\frac{n(n-1)}2+Cn,\qquad A,C\in\Z_{>0},
\]
and transition $\ell$ starts at the quadratic integer valued polynomial
\[
 S_\ell(n)=T(n)+\sum_{h<\ell}(h_h(n)+q_h).
\]
The half open cycle intervals $[T(n),T(n+1))$ partition all times from $T_0$ onward. Each transition owns $[S_\ell(n),S_\ell(n)+h_\ell(n)+q_\ell)$.

If its packet period is $p_\ell$, then for each residue $0\le r<p_\ell$ the flight chart has parameters $n,j\in\NN$, domain and time
\begin{equation}\label{smc:zd:eq:halfopen}
 h_\ell(n)-r-p_\ell j-1\ge0,\qquad
 t=S_\ell(n)+r+p_\ell j.
\end{equation}
It owns only the times strictly before first contact. For $0\le s<q_\ell$, a local prefix chart has parameter $n$ and time $S_\ell(n)+h_\ell(n)+s$. If $q_\ell=0$, contact is the next checkpoint and belongs to the next transition. Thus no boundary is counted twice.

The cycle interval determines $n$, the transition interval determines $\ell$, and division determines the unique flight phase and $j$ or the unique local prefix phase. This proves one chart point per exact time, even if the spatial configuration repeats. Stationary frame coordinates $g_v$ are affine in the chart parameters, and original frame coordinates
\[
 P_v=g_v+\delta t
\]
have degree at most two. If $J$ denotes the raw chart count, the literal construction gives $J=T_0+p$ for an affine tail and $J=T_0+\sum_\ell(p_\ell+q_\ell)$ for a shuttle. These input dependent quantities may be enormous.

\subsection{Disjoint lexicographic sorting and its cost}
For a raw chart with $m$ occupied sites, refine by every permutation $\pi$ and every list of first differing coordinates $k_a\in\{1,\ldots,d\}$ for adjacent sites $v=\pi(a)$, $w=\pi(a+1)$. Require
\[
 g_{w,h}-g_{v,h}=0\quad(h<k_a),\qquad
 g_{w,k_a}-g_{v,k_a}-1\ge0.
\]
The common drift cancels in pair differences, so these conditions are affine. Occupied sites are distinct; hence exactly one order and one first difference list apply at each chart point. Equal labels do not cause multiplicity. Use positive denominator clearing wherever needed.

The sorting factor is $1$ when $m\le1$, and $m!d^{m-1}$ otherwise. Let $B_{\rm ch}$ be the retained chart count after any fixed label tuple filtering, $K$ its total private parameter slots, $M$ its domain inequalities, and $H$ its domain equalities. Then
\begin{equation}\label{smc:zd:eq:chartcounts}
 B_{\rm ch}\le24d^3J,\quad K\le2B_{\rm ch},\quad
 M\le4B_{\rm ch},\quad H\le3(d-1)B_{\rm ch}.
\end{equation}
The notation $B_{\rm ch}$ is separate from the geometric prefix bound $B$ in the main proof. Incompatible label tuples simply retain no charts.

\subsection{The inherited constant term lifting compiler}
A slightly more general interface also covers the pattern consequences below. Chart $i$ has private natural parameters $z_i$, integer polynomial inequalities $A_{ir}(z_i)\ge0$, integer polynomial equalities $H_{is}(z_i)=0$, and rational output vector $P_i(z_i)\in\Q^Q$. All degrees are at most two; outputs are integer valued on the domain. Assume the disjoint domains map injectively to the intended external outputs.

Introduce a natural selector $e_i$, the private parameters, and one natural slack $u_{ir}$ for every inequality. Write
\[
 E=\sum_i e_i,\qquad Z_i=\sum_jz_{ij}+\sum_ru_{ir},\qquad
 \mathcal L_i(f)=f(z_i)-f(0)+f(0)e_i.
\]
Only the literal constant is multiplied by the selector, so no quadratic monomial becomes cubic. For each output coordinate choose a positive common denominator $L_q$ across \emph{all} chart polynomials. Define
\begin{align*}
 R_0&=E-1,&R_{ir}&=\mathcal L_i(A_{ir})-u_{ir},\\
 U_{is}&=\mathcal L_i(H_{is}),&G_i&=(1-e_i)Z_i,\\
 V_q&=L_q\left(y_q-\sum_i\mathcal L_i(P_{iq})\right).
\end{align*}
All residuals have integer coefficients and ordinary degree at most two in the external and witness variables together. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{smc:zd:eq:sos}
 C(y;w)=R_0^2+\sum_{ir}R_{ir}^2+\sum_{is}U_{is}^2
             +\sum_qV_q^2+\sum_iG_i^2
\end{equation}
is an ordinary quartic sum of squares.

At a natural zero, $E=1$ forces exactly one selector to be $1$. Every inactive gate forces $Z_i=0$, and nonnegativity forces all its parameters and slacks individually to be zero. Its lifted forms vanish. In the selected branch, the remaining equations enforce precisely its domain, its uniquely determined integer slacks, and its output. Conversely an admissible chart point gives that unique zero, with every inactive private variable zero. Thus the natural zeros are in bijection with admissible chart points over the specified output. Half open ownership and disjoint sorting prove Theorem~\ref{smc:zd:thm:quartic}.

\wtag\ [This is the argument of source~18's Proposition~\ref{smc:tq:prop:compiler}, whose residuals~\eqref{smc:tq:eq:domainres}--\eqref{smc:tq:eq:outres} and squared gates~\eqref{smc:tq:eq:sos} appear above as $U_{is}$, $V_q$ and $G_i$; source~19 says itself that the one-dimensional conclusion is not new. The programme has read the shipped companion proof \path{19-mass-four-zd-timed-PROOF.md} in full, in a follow-up (\path{original_frame_first_hit30_fixture.md} with its independent review \path{review_original_frame_first_hit30_fixture.md}, commit \texttt{5b4c091e6})~\cite{reviewfixture}, and found its half-open chart ownership, common clock, first-difference sorting, constant-term lifts, pooled denominators, uniqueness of the inactive natural witnesses and original-frame padding sound as conditional statements; the general normal form stays an inherited hypothesis.]

There are exactly
\begin{equation}\label{smc:zd:eq:witnesscount}
 N_{\rm w}=B_{\rm ch}+K+M\le7B_{\rm ch}\le168d^3J
\end{equation}
natural witnesses, and $1+M+H+Q+B_{\rm ch}$ squared residual slots before deleting identically zero terms. For a fixed $m$ site label tuple, $Q=1+dm$. An empty chart family uses $C=1$ and no witnesses. These counts are deliberately coarse and input dependent.

The polynomial is nonnegative on all real arguments, but its exactness and witness uniqueness are assertions over \emph{natural} witnesses. Replacing them by unrestricted signed difference pairs breaks the inactive branch argument. Pooled denominators are essential: separately scaled branch outputs cannot simply be added without a common scale.

\subsection{Whole configurations and signed external coordinates}
One polynomial can cover varying site counts and labels by encoding
\[
 (t,m,a_1,\ldots,a_4,X_1,\ldots,X_4)
\]
with positive integer nonzero label codes, occupied sites sorted, and all unused label and coordinate slots zero. Append those constants to each chart output; then $Q=6+4d$ and the same witness bound applies. Padded zero coordinates remain literally zero and do not acquire the drift $\delta t$.

For equality up to translation, subtract the first occupied site. The relative coordinates $P_v-P_1=g_v-g_1$ are affine, and time remains quadratic. There is no translation witness. The vacuum is handled separately rather than granting it infinitely many arbitrary translation witnesses.

Signed external integer coordinates are valid polynomial inputs. If all external slots must be natural, replace each scalar $X$ by $X^+-X^-$ and add the squared residual $(X^+X^-)^2$. The accepted pair is uniquely $(\max(X,0),\max(-X,0))$. These are external slots, not extra witnesses. Total degree remains at most four.

\subsection{Fixed patterns including prescribed zeros}
For integer vectors $v,w$, there are $2d+1$ disjoint comparison outcomes: equality, or either sign at their first differing coordinate. Equality uses $d$ coordinate equations; each strict outcome uses earlier coordinate equalities and one inequality with an integer margin of $1$. Comparisons of quadratic outputs therefore introduce only degree two domain conditions, within the compiler interface.

For a fixed anchored pattern on $s$ sites, refine every sorted orbit chart by all comparisons of each occupied site with each window site. The full comparison matrix and fixed labels decide its truth, including zeros. Retain successful matrices and output only $t$. The refinement is disjoint, and the orbit determines a unique chart point at each time, so the quartic has an empty or singleton natural witness fiber. One may take
\[
 B_{\rm anch}\le B_{\rm ch}(2d+1)^{4s},\qquad
 N_{\rm anch}\le(7+4s)B_{\rm anch}.
\]
Here $B_{\rm ch}$ refers to all sorted orbit charts before observation filtering.

For a translated pattern with a nonzero prescribed site $w_*$, every successful placement sends $w_*$ to one of at most four occupied sites carrying the correct label. The candidate shift is $b_i=P_i-w_*$. Refine by the \emph{complete} matrix of comparisons
\[
 P_v-P_i\quad\hbox{against}\quad w-w_*
\]
for all occupied $i,v$ and all pattern sites $w$. The drift cancels and these conditions are affine. The matrix determines the success of every candidate. Retain the matrix once if any candidate succeeds, rather than introducing one branch per success. If a placement is also output, choose the least successful occupied site index. This prevents same time placement multiplicity. Bounds are
\[
 B_{\rm trans}\le B_{\rm ch}(2d+1)^{16s},\qquad
 N_{\rm trans}\le(7+16s)B_{\rm trans}.
\]

A wholly zero translated pattern, including the empty pattern, occurs in every finite support configuration at every time. For occurrence in external natural $t$ alone, the zero polynomial with no witnesses has the single empty witness tuple. There need not be a lexicographically least placement. If a placement is wanted, choose a specified rule that moves its entire window beyond the maximal first support coordinate, with the vacuum handled separately.

These are fixed pattern statements. The chart family and counts may depend on the whole pattern. No uniform arity claim is made for an arbitrary pattern provided as one encoded external input.

\subsection{What time uniqueness does not imply}
Time is external in every single witness claim. If it is existentially quantified instead, the same construction has one zero for \emph{each occurrence time}. A stationary nonempty configuration already gives an infinite fiber. Thus this appendix does not establish untimed finite foldness, a generic single fold MRDP theorem, a uniform bound over input configurations, or exactness for rational or real witnesses. The explicit chart interface, not bare decidability, is what permits the quartic compiler.

The companion finite checks cover 208 drift restored two dimensional shuttle states, 325 lexicographic refinement cases, 1,215 finite witness cube evaluations, and additional selectors, inactive gates, domains, signed pairs, and pattern placements. Their normal and optimized receipts agree. They test the displayed algebra on fixtures; they do not implement a general CA to chart compiler or prove the dynamical normal form by experiment.

\section{Part~VII: canonical first visit quartic certificates (source~20)}\label{smc:og:app:quartic}
The first arrival theorem yields a direct Diophantine consequence that avoids counting repeated visits as different witnesses. We give the construction rather than invoking a generic finite fold or single fold representation theorem. All witnesses below are natural numbers, including zero; the external site coordinates may be signed integers.

\begin{theorem}[One canonical witness per visited site]\label{smc:og:thm:quartic}
For a fixed promised CA and fixed mass at most four input, an ordinary integer polynomial $P(y;w)$ of total degree at most four can be effectively constructed such that
\[
 \#\{w\in\NN^W:P(y;w)=0\}=
 \begin{cases}1,&y\in S,\\0,&y\notin S,\end{cases}
 \qquad y\in\Z^d.
\]
The stationary frame first visit time $\tau(y)$ is canonically reconstructible from the unique witness. Alternatively, one can enforce it as an external argument $t$ or move that time argument into one additional canonical natural witness, still at total degree at most four. The polynomial, coefficients, and witness arity depend on this rule and input.
\end{theorem}
The stronger form with a time argument has a unique natural witness exactly when $y\in S$ and $t=\tau(y)$, and none otherwise. Time is actual elapsed discrete time, not a cycle number. A certificate for all visits followed by existentially forgetting time would not prove this: recurrent sites could retain many or infinitely many time witnesses.

\subsection{Disjoint clauses for the spatial domain}
More generally, take any effective Presburger set $S\subseteq\Z^d$ with a specified nonnegative integer function $f$ represented by rational quadratic polynomials on finitely many semilinear cells. The first arrival theorem supplies this data for an expanding tail and its prefix. For independent or translated periodic tails the timed occurrence relation is already Presburger; least time selection and Lemma~\ref{smc:og:lem:affine} give piecewise rational affine $f=\tau$. Finite and empty sets are included.

Apply effective Presburger elimination to all cell formulas. Collect their integer affine comparison forms $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_a$ and congruence expressions $g_j$ modulo fixed positive $m_j$, $1\le j\le q$. Enumerate the complete outcomes: each $\ell$ is negative, zero, or positive, and each $g_j$ has exactly one of its $m_j$ residues. Retain an outcome exactly when it belongs to at least one original cell and assign the polynomial of its least indexed containing cell. All such polynomials give $f$ on that outcome. Unsatisfiable outcomes may be deleted.

Converting strict signs by integer unit offsets yields pairwise disjoint branches $i$ of the form
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:clauses}
 A_{ir}(y)\ge0,\qquad H_{is}(y)=0,\qquad
 L_{iv}(y)\equiv r_{iv}\pmod{m_{iv}},
\end{equation}
with integer affine forms, $m_{iv}>0$, and normalized residues. This partitions spatial points, not coefficients of a semilinear generating family. No uniqueness of those generator coefficients is needed.

Let $B$ be the number of branches and let $I,H,C$ be the total occurrences of inequality, equality, and congruence atoms in them. Before redundant atoms are removed, the full outcome construction gives
\[
 B\le3^a\prod_{j=1}^q m_j,\qquad I+H=aB,\qquad C=qB.
\]
The potentially enormous size is not hidden by the constant degree assertion.

\subsection{Affine gating with canonical signed quotients}
For each branch introduce a natural selector $e_i$. For each inequality introduce one natural slack $u_{ir}$, and for each congruence introduce two natural quotient variables $q_{iv}^+,q_{iv}^-$. Form the residuals
\begin{align}
 R_0&=\sum_i e_i-1,\notag\\
 R_{ir}&=e_iA_{ir}(y)-u_{ir},\notag\\
 S_{is}&=e_iH_{is}(y),\label{smc:og:eq:membership-residuals}\\
 Q_{iv}&=e_i\bigl(L_{iv}(y)-r_{iv}\bigr)
             -m_{iv}(q_{iv}^+-q_{iv}^-),\notag\\
 K_{iv}&=q_{iv}^+q_{iv}^-.\notag
\end{align}
Let $P$ be the sum of all their squares. Each residual has integer coefficients and total degree at most two, including the external $y$ coordinates, so $P$ has ordinary total degree at most four.

At a natural zero, $R_0=0$ selects exactly one branch. On an inactive branch, $R_{ir}=0$ forces its slack to zero. Its congruence balance forces $q^+=q^-$, and their zero product forces both to zero. Thus no inactive auxiliary remains free. On the active branch the slack is uniquely $A_{ir}(y)$, the equalities hold, and each congruence fixes an integer quotient
\[
 q=\bigl(L_{iv}(y)-r_{iv}\bigr)/m_{iv}.
\]
The zero product forces its unique natural encoding $(q^+,q^-)=(\max(q,0),\max(-q,0))$. The disjoint clauses determine the selector uniquely. Conversely these canonical assignments make every residual vanish for each $y\in S$. A sum of real squares is zero exactly when every residual is zero, proving membership and witness uniqueness.

For $B>0$ the literal witness and squared residual counts are
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:memcounts}
 W_{\rm mem}=B+I+2C,\qquad R_{\rm mem}=1+I+H+2C.
\end{equation}
For an empty branch list use $P=1$ and no witnesses.

\wtag\ [Second route. This canonical semilinear membership quartic is Part~IV's compiler of Section~\ref{smc:sl:sec:presburger-quartic} (a unique slack for each inequality and the sign-separated Euclidean quotient~\eqref{smc:sl:eq:congruence}) combined with Part~I's selector device (Theorem~\ref{smc:cg:thm:quarticunion}); Part~IV gates its clauses through a circuit of truth bits, whereas source~20 multiplies each atom by its selector. Part~XI of \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates} gives every semilinear set a single-fold representation of degree two that is nonnegative on the real orthant (Theorem \texttt{cdc:of:thm:singlefoldsl}; compare Remark~\ref{smc:cg:rem:singlefold}), so degree four is not needed for membership alone. No novelty is claimed for this subsection. New in this appendix are the one shared cycle variable for quadratic first arrivals, the square lifts for general piecewise quadratic time, the untimed first-hit certificates and the original-frame complete-target certificate through whole-orbit injectivity.]

\subsection{One shared cycle variable for actual first arrivals}
The expanding shuttle application admits a sharper clock implementation than a general quadratic function. Retain on each final tail clause its rational affine functions $n_i(y)=n_*(y)$ and $k_i(y)=T_0+h_*(y)$. On every prefix first visit singleton, set $n_i=0$ and $k_i$ equal to its first time. All clauses then share
\[
 \tau(y)=cn_i(y)^2+bn_i(y)+k_i(y),
\]
with the same $c,b$, and both affine functions are nonnegative integers on their assigned cells.

Introduce one shared natural variable $N$. Choose positive integers $A,D$ so that $a_i=An_i$ and $K_i=Dk_i$ are integer affine, and $Dc,Db$ are integers. Add exactly the two squared residuals
\begin{align}\label{smc:og:eq:sharedcycle}
 AN-\sum_i e_i a_i(y),\qquad
 Dt-DcN^2-DbN-\sum_i e_iK_i(y).
\end{align}
The unique selector fixes $N=n_i(y)$ and the second residual fixes $t=\tau(y)$. Both residuals have ordinary total degree at most two, so their squares retain degree at most four. Prefix branches work with $N=0$, without negative offsets or an extra selector. All inactive membership auxiliaries were already forced to zero.

Thus for actual expanding first arrivals, time external requires
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:sharedcounts}
 W=B+I+2C+1,\qquad R=3+I+H+2C.
\end{equation}
Making time itself a natural witness changes the first count to $B+I+2C+2$, with the same residual count. These are counts of variables in the unique witness tuple, not a claim that the tuple has just one variable. Only the extra clock overhead is dimension independent; the clause and atom counts still depend on the full fixed rule and input. Reconstruction of first time from the membership witness alone needs no added variable.

\subsection{General piecewise quadratic time outputs}
At the unique membership witness, the selected branch already gives the canonical expression $f(y)=\sum_i e_i f_i(y)$. Its total degree may be three, which is harmless for reconstruction but would produce degree six if squared as a residual. To preserve degree four when enforcing time, use shared natural square lifts
\[
 U_j=y_j^2\quad(1\le j\le d),\qquad
 U_{jk}=(y_j+y_k)^2\quad(1\le j<k\le d).
\]
Impose these with their quadratic difference residuals. The universal sufficient number is $M=d(d+1)/2$, and each lift is uniquely nonnegative even for signed coordinates. Polarization gives
\[
 y_jy_k=\tfrac12(U_{jk}-U_j-U_k).
\]
Thus every $f_i$ becomes a rational affine expression in $(y,U)$. Choose one common positive denominator $D$, including the polarization halves, and write it as $F_i(y,U)/D$ with integer affine $F_i$. Add the single pooled residual
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:time-residual}
 Dt-\sum_i e_iF_i(y,U).
\end{equation}
It has total degree at most two. Adding its square and the square lift residuals to $P$ retains degree at most four and forces exactly $t=f(y)$. All shared lift values are canonical independently of branch selection; none are left as inactive degrees of freedom.

For external time the literal counts are
\begin{equation}\label{smc:og:eq:timecounts}
 W_{\rm time}=B+I+2C+M,\qquad
 R_{\rm time}=2+I+H+2C+M.
\end{equation}
Moving $t$ to a natural witness adds one to $W$ and leaves $R$ unchanged. For affine cell functions one may take $M=0$. Unused square lifts can be omitted after specifying a sufficient list.

If all external slots must also be natural, replace each $y_j$ by $Y_j^+-Y_j^-$ and add the squared product residual $(Y_j^+Y_j^-)^2$. This accepts exactly the canonical positive and negative part pair. It changes $d$ signed external slots to $2d$ natural slots, adds $d$ residuals, and adds no witnesses. Affine substitution and quadratic products preserve the degree bound.

\subsection{First hits of configurations and fixed patterns}
The same construction supplies a useful untimed reachability refinement of Report 29 \textup{[source~19]}. Fix a finite tuple of external observation parameters $z$, and a time independent observation predicate that is Presburger in $z$ and the finite labeled support of the stationary frame configuration. The schema and tuple length are fixed when compiling the polynomial.

Substituting the finite phase support lists produces a Presburger occurrence relation $\operatorname{Occ}(z,n,h)$ in half open expanding cycles. It may have many witnesses; only its truth is used. A prefix time can satisfy infinitely many $z$, so the site-only singleton treatment must be replaced. If $P_t(z)$ is the observation predicate evaluated on the actual prefix configuration at time $t$, assign that time to the Presburger domain
\[
 P_t(z)\ \wedge\ \bigwedge_{0\le s<t}\neg P_s(z).
\]
These finitely many domains are disjoint and cover all prefix hits. Exclude their union before minimizing $(n,h)$ on the tail. Least cycle and offset are again Presburger functions and become rational affine on effective semilinear cells. Prefix cells use $n_i=0,k_i=t$; tail cells use $n_i=n_*(z),k_i=T_0+h_*(z)$. The shared counter construction~\eqref{smc:og:eq:sharedcycle} therefore applies unchanged.

\begin{corollary}[Canonical untimed first hit certificates]\label{smc:og:cor:firsthit}
For every such fixed schema, its stationary frame first hit domain has an ordinary integer polynomial of degree at most four with exactly one natural witness tuple per accepted external observation and none otherwise. Its first absolute hit time is canonically reconstructible, external, or one additional natural witness. The counts in~\eqref{smc:og:eq:memcounts} and~\eqref{smc:og:eq:sharedcounts} apply to its final disjoint clauses. This includes the following observations.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Complete finite configuration targets, canonically encoded by a site count $0\le m\le4$, positive codes for active nonzero labels from the fixed alphabet, and at most four signed coordinate slots, strictly lexicographically sorted, with zero label and coordinate padding beyond $m$.
\item Anchors of a fixed finite pattern, including prescribed vacuum symbols. The anchor is external; the pattern's finite offsets and symbols are fixed at compilation.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}
For complete targets, cardinality, labels, coordinate equality with all phase support sites, ordering, and padding are finite Presburger conditions. Thus actual untimed stationary frame configuration reachability has a unique natural witness tuple for every reachable canonical target. This does not follow merely by dropping the time argument from an all occurrence certificate.

For a fixed pattern, each nonzero entry requires a matching occupied site at the anchor plus its offset; each zero entry requires absence of all occupied sites there. This is again a finite Presburger predicate. The argument does not cover an arbitrary unbounded external pattern encoding.

There is no quadratic distance growth assertion for general observation parameters. A wholly zero pattern can already hold at infinitely many distant anchors at time zero. The corollary concerns semilinear first hit domains, piecewise quadratic first times, and canonical quartic certificates. As before, it is a fixed rule, fixed input result, and does not by itself assert general expanding original frame reachability under nonzero drift. Complete targets admit the separate corollary below.

\subsection{Complete configuration targets in the original frame}
For full configurations there is a further argument that removes the frame restriction. It uses exact trajectory injectivity, not a first visit spatial substitution.

\begin{lemma}[An expanding whole configuration orbit is injective]\label{smc:og:lem:orbit-injective}
If the classified tail is expanding, the complete configurations $F^t(c_0)$ are pairwise distinct for all $t\ge0$, including the initial prefix.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
At section $n$ the actual two residual unit sites have separation $(x_0+n\Delta)\nu+z$, with a fixed transverse offset $z$. Hence the configuration diameter is at least $\inorm{(x_0+n\Delta)\nu+z}$ and tends to infinity. Translating by $T_n\delta$ leaves this diameter unchanged, so this statement holds in the original frame.

If $F^a(c_0)=F^b(c_0)$ for $a<b$, determinism implies $F^{a+s}(c_0)=F^{b+s}(c_0)$ for every $s\ge0$. The future then consists of finitely many repeated configurations and has bounded diameter. This contradicts the unbounded section diameters. The argument excludes every repeated pair, even if one or both times precede the eventual tail.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Original frame untimed configuration certificate]\label{smc:og:cor:original-target}
For every fixed promised CA and input, original frame untimed complete configuration reachability admits an effective ordinary integer polynomial of degree at most four with exactly one natural witness tuple for each reachable canonical target and none otherwise. The first reachability time can be part of that tuple.

For an expanding tail, the timed chart construction of Report 29 \textup{[source~19, Appendix~\ref{smc:zd:app:quartic}, whose compiler is source~18's]} gives the explicit bound
\[
 W\le7B_{\rm ch}+1,
\]
where $B_{\rm ch}$ is its retained sorted timed chart count, not the Presburger clause count $B$ in this appendix. No uniform over input arity bound is asserted.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Report 29's Appendix A \textup{[source~19's Appendix~\ref{smc:zd:app:quartic}, bound~\eqref{smc:zd:eq:witnesscount}]} supplies a quartic $C(t,z;w)$ for canonical complete original frame targets $z$, with exactly one natural witness tuple for each actual time and target pair, and at most $7B_{\rm ch}$ witness variables. Its half open charts, lexicographic sorting, and inactive branch gates establish that uniqueness. Reclassify the external elapsed time $t$ as one additional natural witness. Lemma~\ref{smc:og:lem:orbit-injective} says that a reachable complete target has exactly one occurrence time. Therefore its combined tuple $(t,w)$ is unique, and total degree remains at most four. The time is automatically its first occurrence.

For independent or whole translated periodic tails, the full original frame timed occurrence relation is Presburger: restoring drift preserves the affine phase rays. Select the least occurrence time and apply the disjoint clause construction with a piecewise affine time output. Prefix overlaps and repeated periodic configurations are handled by this first time selection. Finite and empty cases are included.
\end{proof}
This argument is specific to complete configurations. Sites, labels, and pattern observations can recur even when the entire configuration never repeats. The original frame extensions for their first visits therefore do not follow from this corollary. In particular it does not repair a substitution of $y-\delta t$ into a stationary site first visit certificate.

\subsection{Application and limits}
For expanding shuttles, first remove the finitely many sites first visited in the prefix and then select the lexicographically least cycle and within cycle offset using the Presburger occurrence relation. Equation~\eqref{smc:og:eq:firstarrival} supplies the quadratic cell function. This proves Theorem~\ref{smc:og:thm:quartic} for the entire stationary frame set, not just its eventual tail. Affine, periodic, independent, and finite alternatives use their affine first time functions. Vacuum uses the empty polynomial certificate $P=1$.

If $\delta=0$, the result directly concerns $F$. For affine tails, restoring $\delta t$ preserves affine timed phase rays, so the same construction also works in the original frame. For expanding site and pattern observations, no general original frame first hit corollary is asserted. Complete configurations have the separate argument of Corollary~\ref{smc:og:cor:original-target}. Substituting $y-\delta t$ into a stationary first visit certificate is insufficient: a later return to a stationary frame site can create a new original frame site that the first visit certificate omits.

This is a direct elementary construction using disjoint Presburger clauses and explicit square lifting. It is not a generic single fold MRDP theorem, an inference from decidability alone, an assertion for rational or real witnesses, or a uniform over input arity bound. The full supporting note and its independent review are included in the scientific sources. \wtag\ [Shipped as \path{20-orbit-geometry-first-visit-PROOF.md}, \path{20-orbit-geometry-first-visit-independent-review.md}, \path{20-orbit-geometry-original-frame-PROOF.md} and \path{20-orbit-geometry-original-frame-REVIEW.md}.] Finite algebra fixtures check the formula bookkeeping, inactive branch zeros, signed quotients, time lift, and degree counts; they are not a general Presburger elimination or CA classification implementation.

\wtag\ \textbf{The programme's intake.} The Hilbert's-tenth programme read the shipped original-frame proof and first-visit companion in full, and the central parts of the geometry proof, in \path{review_original_frame_first_hit30_intake.md} (\path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/research-wip/native-stream-queue/}, commit \texttt{c875bad40}, 3 October 2026)~\cite{reviewfirsthit}. It found no defect in the step of Corollary~\ref{smc:og:cor:original-target} that turns the timed certificate into an untimed one through whole-orbit injectivity (Lemma~\ref{smc:og:lem:orbit-injective}), nor in the separate treatment of affine and periodic tails. It is an intake, not a review of the compiler: it did not review the inherited timed certificate of source~19's Appendix~\ref{smc:zd:app:quartic} (whose companion proof it names as the next audit), and it stresses that the witness and residual counts here are not gate counts, that the bound $W\le7B_{\rm ch}+1$, with $B_{\rm ch}\le24d^3J$ (source~19's~\eqref{smc:zd:eq:chartcounts}), depends on the whole automaton and input, and that the shared cycle variable is a lead for a paid comparison, not an established saving. It executed no archived code. In a follow-up (\path{original_frame_first_hit30_fixture.md} with its independent review \path{review_original_frame_first_hit30_fixture.md}, commit \texttt{5b4c091e6})~\cite{reviewfixture}, the programme then read source~19's timed proof in full and found no gap in it, conditional on the normal form, and, for one drifted orbit of the weighted planar automaton of Section~\ref{smc:og:sec:wedge} composed with a unit vertical shift, emitted complete first-hit quartics for complete original-frame targets, the smallest with 35 paid operations (11 multiplications, 24 additions), three natural witnesses, eight squared rows and exact degree four. That is one fixed-input certificate in a decidable class, not a compiler, an arity bound over inputs or a change of the universal bounds.
